# T3 handoff — Rigid, RFT, MPM-Flat and MPM-Rough locomotion baselines

> **For the lead session:** changes requested to `tasks/T3.md` are in the section "Requested changes to `tasks/T3.md`, for the lead session (2026-11-21)" at the end of this file.

## 1. Scope: what this session worked on for this topic.

Inspection timestamp: **2026-11-15T21:00:40.172192+00:00**. This is a documentation-only handoff from `/home/jkamohara/isaac/IsaacLab`.

The user requested “Step 3 only: write the handoff.” `misc/T3_migration.md` actually puts handoff writing in Step 1 and handoff verification in Step 3. `outputs/T3.md` did not exist. This document fulfills the explicit request to write the handoff and includes Step 3's acceptance-criteria assessment and proposed plan. No worktree, commit, code edit, new evaluation, dependency change, training submission, or cancellation was performed for this request. Existing previously authorized jobs were not stopped or restarted.

Relevant work in this long conversation:

- Built and iterated G1 rigid/RFT flat and rough training baselines; compared PhysX/Newton recipes and policies, corrected contact/asset issues, and introduced standalone `*_mpm_align` tasks.
- Submitted baseline training with W&B/video, then evaluated deterministic policies on native fixed-bed MPM and a rigid counterpart: slope traversal, stance on a tilting sand tray, and continuous density/friction changes.
- Retained archived failures and newer replacements. Old and new run IDs, checkpoints, backend versions and assets must not be treated as interchangeable.
- Current completed comparison contains **three**, not four, policies: Rigid-rough `3qymp37c`, RFT-rough `sfwpm6xj`, MPM-rough `jqur5jhm`. A matched MPM-Flat trained policy is **unknown / not identified**. Evaluating a rough-trained policy on a flat bed does not create an MPM-Flat training ablation.

T3's primary question is hardware transfer. Simulation is supporting evidence, not an unbiased referee for MPM training. No hardware comparison is established by the artifacts inspected here. Moving-patch performance/sleeping/cache work belongs to T1; NeRD and other embodiments are outside this handoff.

## 3. Commands and configs: the exact commands, task IDs, and config files used for each result.

**Do not run the commands during handoff verification.** Documented training recipes are distinguished from preserved evaluation command records. Exact historical submission invocations for all run IDs are **unknown** without the corresponding OSMO snapshot; a README command is not proof of what a past job executed.

Current registrations, verified in each task's `__init__.py`:

| Role | Task ID | Config |
|---|---|---|
| Aligned rigid rough | `IsaacContrib-Velocity-Rough-G1-29dof-Rigid-MPMAlign` | `g1_29dof_rigid_mpm_align/rough_env_cfg.py:G1RoughEnvCfg` |
| Aligned rigid flat | `IsaacContrib-Velocity-Flat-G1-29dof-Rigid-MPMAlign` | same package `flat_env_cfg.py:G1FlatEnvCfg` |
| Aligned RFT rough | `IsaacContrib-Velocity-Rough-G1-29dof-SoftContact-MPMAlign` | `g1_29dof_soft_contact_mpm_align/rough_env_cfg.py:G1RoughEnvCfg` |
| Aligned RFT flat | `IsaacContrib-Velocity-Flat-G1-29dof-SoftContact-MPMAlign` | same package `flat_env_cfg.py:G1FlatEnvCfg` |
| MPM mixed training | `IsaacContrib-Velocity-G1-29dof-MPM-MovingPatch-MixedTerrain` | `velocity_mpm/g1_29dof_mpm_moving_patch/mixed_env_cfg.py:G1MixedTerrainEnvCfg` |
| Pure moving-patch MPM | `IsaacContrib-Velocity-Sand-G1-29dof-MPM-MovingPatch` | same package `mpm_env_cfg.py:G1MovingPatchEnvCfg` |

All register `-Play` variants. The four aligned rigid/RFT tasks use built-in `ManagerBasedRLEnv`; their component files are in `env_cfg/` and standard PPO is in `agents/rsl_rl_ppo_cfg.py`. MPM has a custom environment for coupling/particle management. Its pure and mixed task default agent entry points are different; inspect registration before assuming standard vs compositional PPO.

Documented latest rough commands, from the two aligned-task READMEs (fresh training, four GPUs, 1,024 environments/GPU, seed 42, video/W&B):

```bash
cd /home/jkamohara/isaac/IsaacLab
bash osmo/run_multinode.sh osmo/osmo_multi_gpu.yaml \
  --pool isaac-lab-l40-07 \
  --set num_nodes=1 num_gpu=4 num_cpu=32 memory=192 \
  --set-string \
    workflow_name=isaac-lab-g1-rigid-rough-mpm-align \
    platform=ovx-l40 \
    output_url=swift://pdx.s8k.io/AUTH_team-isaac-sqa/isaac-sqa/jkamohara/contact-benchmark/runs \
    wandb_credential=wb-auth wandb_key=wb_api_key \
    args="--task IsaacContrib-Velocity-Rough-G1-29dof-Rigid-MPMAlign --num_envs 1024 --seed 42 --logger wandb --wandb_project g1_29dof_contact_benchmark --run_name rigid_rough_mpm_align_box --viz newton_gl --video"

bash osmo/run_multinode.sh osmo/osmo_multi_gpu.yaml \
  --pool isaac-lab-l40-07 \
  --set num_nodes=1 num_gpu=4 num_cpu=32 memory=192 \
  --set-string \
    workflow_name=isaac-lab-g1-rft-rough-mpm-align \
    platform=ovx-l40 \
    output_url=swift://pdx.s8k.io/AUTH_team-isaac-sqa/isaac-sqa/jkamohara/contact-benchmark/runs \
    wandb_credential=wb-auth wandb_key=wb_api_key \
    args="--task IsaacContrib-Velocity-Rough-G1-29dof-SoftContact-MPMAlign --num_envs 1024 --seed 42 --logger wandb --wandb_project g1_29dof_contact_benchmark --run_name rft_rough_mpm_align_box --viz newton_gl --video"
```

Both agent configs use 24 rollout steps, 20,000 iterations, ELU MLP `[512,256,128]`, 29 actions, actor inputs `[policy,command]` (937 values), critic `[critic,command,privileged]`, action scale 0.25, augmentation and mirror loss 0.1. The policy proprioceptive history is 10 samples; commands have no history. The rough generator uses ten rows and ten columns of 8×8 m tiles, with waves, random roughness, positive/inverted pyramid slopes and flat ground. Rigid has surface colliders everywhere. RFT uses even rigid columns and odd soft columns with support depth increasing from 0 to 0.12 m across rows; the analytical surface stays at the upper mesh. Terrain progression changes both roughness and support depth. These are not simply flat beds with renamed IDs.

`osmo/osmo_contact_benchmark.md` contains an **older** 2-GPU × 1,280-environment recipe, earlier compositional PPO variants, cancellations, mirror/separation changes and support-collider corrections. Do not present its opening table as the exact recipe of every subsequent run.

Latest evaluation protocol (from saved `protocol.json`, not the stale README):

- Root: `logs/rss_locomotion/final_rough_slope_20261115`.
- Slope: 0, ±10, ±20, ±25, ±30 degrees; native generated 6×4 m ramp; seeds 41/42/43; three policies; two evaluation surfaces; **162 trials**. Native upward/downward scene owners are `G1RoughMPMEnvCfg_PLAY` / `G1DescendingMPMEnvCfg_PLAY`, from `velocity_mpm/g1_29dof_mpm/g1_mpm_env_cfg.py`.
- Generator is `util/terrain.py:x_sloped_terrain`. The evaluator sets rise/run to `tan(angle)`; the rigid ramp has two faces and is translated to match the approach top (−0.05 m), avoiding a 5 cm entry lip. The saved mesh height error is zero in inspected +25° protocol.
- Rigid: three environments, one seed each; zero feet, ramp and approach collider margins; friction 1.0 on feet/ramp/approach; **four MJWarp articulation substeps, collision refresh each substep**, interval 0.00125 s. The README saying two substeps is stale for this final protocol.
- MPM: one environment at a time, 2 cm foot margin, 0 terrain margin, native coupling settings. Three parallel wide full-resolution MPM beds previously exhausted local GPU memory. Do not change MPM margins to match rigid.
- Slope/material: 1 m/s commanded forward speed, 0.02 s control period, 13 s maximum. End at first native termination, with terminal state captured before automatic reset. Traversal success means forward exit without another termination. Falls mean `bad_orientation` and/or `base_too_low`; side exits/timeouts are separately reported, not silently counted as physical falls.
- Material: native flat bed 4×2×0.3 m, 0.02 m voxel; linearly interpolate density **1800→640 kg/m³** and friction **0.80→0.73**, or the reverse, during t=2–6 s. Young's modulus and yield pressure stay at 1e15 Pa. It is a global time-varying material, not spatially varying patches or literally rigid-to-deformable physics.
- Stance: `G1TiltingMPMEnvCfg_PLAY`, 3×2×0.3 m bed, zero speed, 30 s horizon; roll 0/10/20° or pitch 10/20° amplitudes, other axis zero, periods 12/17 s, initial hold 2 s then 3 s ramp. Five conditions × three policies × three seeds =45 trials.

Each final slope condition has the exact launch argv in `slope/<surface>_<signed-angle>/command.json`, evaluation config, protocol, log and per-trial metadata. Complete preserved commands:

<details><summary>18 recorded slope commands</summary>

```bash
# mpm_+0

Never infer a run ID from `rft` alone. `archive.py` and older reports concern superseded custom beds; don't blindly rerun them as native evaluations.

## 5. Results so far: numbers with units. Mark each as "measured in this session" or "not verified".

**No new rollout was measured for this handoff.** The following are **not verified** experimental results: existing raw metadata/CSV were read and counts recomputed, but physics, policy correctness and training provenance were not independently rerun. This label does not mean the files are absent.

Slope aggregate, 9 angles ×3 seeds per policy/surface (descriptive only; use per-angle tables/plots for interpretation):

| Evaluation surface | Policy/run | Trials | Traversal successes | Recorded physical falls | Evidence status |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| mpm | mpm / `jqur5jhm` | 27 | 26/27 | 1/27 | not verified |
| mpm | rft_rough / `sfwpm6xj` | 27 | 18/27 | 9/27 | not verified |
| mpm | rigid / `3qymp37c` | 27 | 18/27 | 9/27 | not verified |
| rigid | mpm / `jqur5jhm` | 27 | 24/27 | 1/27 | not verified |
| rigid | rft_rough / `sfwpm6xj` | 27 | 27/27 | 0/27 | not verified |
| rigid | rigid / `3qymp37c` | 27 | 27/27 | 0/27 | not verified |

The MPM policy's rigid-surface remainder is two timeouts. Success and fall are different metrics. Do not equate boundary censoring with surviving the full remaining horizon. Three seeds per condition are a small pilot, and one training seed/checkpoint per method is not training-replicate uncertainty.

Latest material sweep: 18/18 complete, each policy 6/6 forward traversals. Mean horizontal velocity-error magnitude over the report's selected bed samples, then averaged per trial: rigid **0.12185 m/s**, RFT **0.46753 m/s**, MPM **0.14031 m/s** (all **not verified**). RFT/MPM trials exit after approximately **4.98–5.76 s**, before the t=6 s schedule endpoint; rigid exits after **7.14–8.04 s**. Thus all three succeed at traversal, but RFT/MPM do **not** complete the full material transition. This prevents claiming full-range adaptation from those traces.

Read-time completion snapshot: slope **162/162**, material **18/18**, stance **12/45**. Stance is still changing under previously authorized workers; read `material_stance_status.json` for later state. Its complete comparison is **not yet verified** here. No aggregate final stance claim is made.

Metric definitions currently used:

| Quantity | Definition / limit |
|---|---|
| Horizontal tracking error [m/s] | Mean norm of yaw-aligned horizontal velocity minus commanded XY velocity; not “RMSE” and not just forward velocity. Report mask is t≥1 s and root X≥bed lower X+0.2 m. |
| Action rate [rad/s] | RMS of finite differences of policy actions ×0.25 /0.02 s; commanded joint-target rate, not actual joint velocity. |
| Torque RMS [N·m] | RMS of sampled applied joint torque. Not cost of transport. |
| Success/fall | All-trial bars; tracking/action curves use successful traversals only. A method with no successes has no tracking curve there, not zero error. |
| CoM drift [m] | Mass-weighted body CoM in tray frame, horizontal distance from t=2 s reference; excludes initial settling. |
| Sole lift [m] | Ankle-roll position plus rotated `(0,0,-0.03539)` m; subtract interpolated minima of sustained force-contact segments (>5 N for ≥2 samples), clamp negative values to zero. This is estimated lift above stance level, **not measured sand-surface clearance or sinkage**. |
| Foot force [N] | World-frame rigid+MPM contact force sampled at 50 Hz; plots use Fz and norm(Fx,Fy) on the flat material bed. Not a physics-rate landing-impact peak. |

Current figure design: rigid red `#D3504F`, RFT green `#009E73`, MPM blue `#0072B2`; solid lines; concise labels `rigid/rft/mpm`; success/fall grouped bars. Material uses one representative trace per policy, zero-referenced sole lift and no base-height panel. Stance titles include both roll and pitch, and zero-tilt tray plots use −10…10° bounds. These design choices do not change physical outcomes.

Step-3 acceptance-criteria assessment against `tasks/T3.md`:

| Acceptance criterion | Status | Evidence / missing work |
|---|---|---|
| 1. Simulation success, velocity **and yaw** tracking per policy with episode count | **Partial** | Completed three-policy slope pilot with counts and horizontal error. Latest summary does not report yaw error; matched fourth MPM-Flat policy is missing. |
| 2. Sinkage, slip, clearance, gait timing, pelvis acceleration, cost of transport for every policy | **Partial** | Raw forces/feet/base velocity/actions/torques exist; lift proxy and old gait calculations exist. Complete validated six-metric reporting for latest four matched policies is absent. |
| 3. Hardware table by policy, commanded speed, trial count/failure | **Not started in evidence available here** | Hardware success/velocity data unknown; no such dataset inspected. |
| 4. Metric-backed mechanism linking gait difference to baseline failure, or explicit none-found | **Partial investigation; not established** | Support-collider and asset bugs were diagnosed historically, but they do not establish a material-model generalization mechanism for latest matched policies. None established here. |
| 5. Report parity if baselines match MPM on hardware | **Not assessable / not started** | No hardware comparison; must remain an explicit reporting requirement. |

## 6. Failed attempts: what was tried and did not work, and why.

- Earlier custom testbeds were rejected by the user. They changed unauthorized geometry/test conditions. Their metrics are archived and must not be mixed with native regenerated data.
- Early narrow-bed side exits were conflated with locomotion failure. Width was increased to 4 m for slopes; outcomes now distinguish forward completion, physical falls, side/backward exits and timeouts. Side exits censor later behavior, so “not fallen yet” is not proof of eventual success.
- Earlier rigid slope configurations had an approach/ramp mismatch and foot-catching behavior. Final saved protocols use the native generated planar ramp, entrance height matching, zero margins and more frequent rigid contact updates. Old plots/videos are not validation of the final collider setup.
- Three parallel full-resolution wide MPM beds caused OOM on the local ~32 GB GPU. Rigid uses three environments; MPM retains sequential seeds. Concurrent unrelated GPU jobs also affected runtime. No throughput claim is made from these evaluation wall times.
- Old RFT rough run `05rx8mub` lacked actual rigid-support geometry in Newton: support was authored outside the imported `/World/ground` root. Moving it under `/World/ground/support` fixed import. `osmo_contact_benchmark.md` records a sphere-drop regression; earlier geometry-only checks missed the issue. This invalidates treating that old shuffling policy as a clean RFT baseline.
- Historical actor-loading mismatches included command history, quaternion order and joint/action dimensions. The benchmark loader checks actual saved architecture and maps commands. `hiwy2c7j`/`kyfj909z` have command history (1,000 actor inputs); later no-history actors have 937. These adaptations are not evidence that all reward/event/asset settings match.
- Standard `.usd`, converted `.usda`, four-sphere and box-foot assets were mixed across experiments. USDA pelvis-contour/hip-pitch collision exclusions were later added. The file extension alone is not the physics difference; exact collision relationships and geometry matter.
- Repeated reward changes (constant pitch vs stance-only pitch, pitch difference, foot separation, sliding, symmetry loss) produced different recipes. Do not attribute all gait differences to the RFT/MPM model.
- The material adaptation sweep ends early for faster traversals. It cannot support an endpoint-adaptation claim despite 100% traversal success.

## 7. Known bugs and open issues.

1. **The current evaluated trio is not fully matched.** Saved `3qymp37c` and `sfwpm6xj` configs have mirror loss 0.1 and `feet_slide=-0.25`. Saved `jqur5jhm` has data augmentation but mirror loss disabled/coefficient 0, and no `feet_slide` term. All use 20,000 iterations and 24 rollout steps, but different environments/process (1,024 vs64) and unknown verified historical global world sizes mean equal training budget is not established.
2. Same box-foot USDA basename across newer runs does not prove identical bytes/collision filters. `jqur5jhm` predates the recent asset correction; checkpoint metadata lacks a demonstrated matching asset hash here.
3. Base height is intentionally 0.75 m above each reward's ground reference. In MPM training that scanner may see buried support 0.25 m below the initial sand surface; rigid/RFT use the upper surface. Thus physical height targets differ despite the same numeric target. Preserve and document this user-approved difference until a new protocol is approved.
4. RFT rough assumptions and local surface treatment need model validation; poor transfer is not automatically an implementation bug or proof of MPM superiority. Existing 3D RFT fixes and force-source selection changed across historical runs; exact snapshots must be retained.
5. README/provenance drift: evaluation README still names old policies; old recipe states 2×1,280 envs; new tasks use 4×1,024; README says two rigid substeps while final protocol says four. `fetch.py` still selects old runs. Do not rerun generic commands assuming they fetch latest actors.
6. Raw data/report copies under ignored `logs/` are essential reproducibility inputs, not disposable generated pages. A clean Git worktree will omit them.
7. Latest stance rerun is incomplete at inspection. Public stance page may still contain old policies pending atomic replacement. Do not publish partial new RFT data alongside old rigid/MPM data as one comparison.
8. Newest source changes may postdate recorded runs. No fresh simulation or complete imported-source/asset hash validation was performed for this handoff; saved source hashes and runtime checks are starting points.
9. No confirmed matched MPM-Flat training, four-policy hardware result, calibrated material parameter fit, or controlled causal gait mechanism is available here.
10. “Rigid with randomized contact compliance” is a **target in T3**, not a verified property of the implemented rigid baseline. Actuator-gain randomization is not compliance randomization of terrain contact.

## 8. Next steps you would take.

**Proposed only; wait for approval before new training or shared-code changes.**

1. Reconcile provenance first: select exact four run IDs/checkpoint hashes and archived training source/assets/lockfiles. Save a parameter-by-parameter intentional-difference table, including symmetry, sliding, physical height targets, world size and total transitions. Resolve the missing MPM-Flat policy.
2. Preserve dirty task/evaluator dependencies and ignored result snapshots before creating any clean worktree. This handoff does not authorize commits or Step 2 migration.
3. Finish/review the already-running latest stance comparison under its existing authorization; retain material/slope originals. Do not relaunch or change the protocol merely to obtain a preferred ranking.
4. Propose a corrected matched training set only after reviewing the differences above. Decide explicitly whether to add contact-compliance randomization, and how to match total transitions rather than iteration count alone.
5. Define missing gait measurements before implementation: sole-to-material surface sinkage, contact-relative tangential slip, terrain-referenced swing clearance, gait events/timing, world-frame pelvis acceleration with gravity/filter definition, and energy convention for cost of transport.
6. Extend analysis from raw arrays where valid; remeasure only missing quantities. Don't rename stance-normalized sole lift to physical sinkage or infer impact peaks from 50 Hz samples. Keep failed-trial statistics visible alongside successful-only tracking.
7. Propose an endpoint-reaching material protocol if full schedule adaptation is required; current early exits should stay labeled as incomplete schedules. Geometry/duration changes require explicit agreement.
8. Complete the deployment/sim-to-sim gate and then a randomized-order hardware comparison across speeds and repeated trials. Hardware, not evaluation in MPM alone, determines the main transfer claim. Report parity or failure of the hypothesis plainly.

## 9. Specific questions:

**Which policies listed in `examples/RSS/locomotion/README.md` were trained with the matched recipe in `osmo/osmo_contact_benchmark.md`, and which predate it?**

None can be certified as an exact match to the current recipe from the inspected evidence. The README is a historical list, not a provenance guarantee:

| Run(s) | Evidence-supported classification |
|---|---|
| `ioo32x36`, `lpwxe5i0` | Older rigid/RFT rough generation. Saved standard `.usd`, 1,024 envs/process and three pitch terms (constant pitch, pitch difference, contact pitch; each −4) differ from the box-foot/stance-only MPM-aligned recipe. Chronology relative to each edit in the older recipe document is unknown. |
| `binkrmei` | Older flat RFT generation with standard `.usd`, no command history, augmentation+mirror loss 0.1 and the same three pitch terms. Not MPM-flat and not latest aligned rough. |
| `hiwy2c7j` | PhysX-trained historical flat RFT recipe, standard `.usd`, command history. Backend/history differ from the matched Newton baseline. |
| `tyiby5k6`, `ufmtmwr9` | Earlier soft-flat PPO comparison policies, box-foot USDA, 4,096 envs/process and three pitch penalties. Saved mirror coefficient 0 vs0.1 respectively. They predate the current aligned tasks. |
| `jqur5jhm` | Historical MPM rough comparator, 64 envs/process, box-foot USDA, stance-angle −8, augmentation without mirror loss and no sliding term. Not a fully matched member of the newer baseline pair. |
| `3qymp37c`, `sfwpm6xj` | Latest evaluated aligned rigid/RFT rough pair, **absent from the old README list**. Both have box-foot USDA, 1,024 envs/process, stance-angle −8, slide −0.25, mirror loss0.1. Their task READMEs are more relevant than the old 2-GPU recipe. They still differ from evaluated `jqur5jhm` as described above. |

The full exact historical chronology and equality of every term/dependency are **unknown** without additional archived snapshot verification. Do not label the set “same training except physics.”

**Does the rigid baseline randomize friction and contact compliance?**

Friction: **yes, configured**. In latest `g1_29dof_rigid_mpm_align/env_cfg/event_cfg.py` and saved `3qymp37c` events, `physics_material` samples ankle-roll body shapes at startup: static friction `[0.6,1.0]`, dynamic friction `[0.2,0.6]`, restitution0, 64 buckets. This is foot material randomization, not demonstrated per-tile terrain-friction randomization. Backend interpretation of the two friction coefficients should be checked before claiming independent static/dynamic dynamics.

Contact compliance: **no event found**. Latest `G1PhysicsCfg` has fixed default shape `ke=160000`, `kd=1100`, margin0; saved default gap0.01 m. There is no sampled contact stiffness/damping/solref/solimp event in the inspected config. Reset randomization scales **actuator** stiffness/damping by `[0.9,1.1]`, which is a different mechanism. Exact MJWarp effective compliance conversion is not revalidated here. The evaluation's fixed rigid friction1.0 and zero margins are separate from training randomization.

**Which gait metrics are already logged (sinkage, slip, clearance, gait timing, pelvis acceleration, cost of transport)?**

| Requested metric | Current evidence | What remains |
|---|---|---|
| Foot sinkage [m] | Sole-position proxy recorded; no actual evolving material surface sampled in latest trace | Not implemented as a validated sinkage metric. Need reference surface, sole point/corners and contact convention. |
| Foot slip [m/s or m] | Foot positions, contact forces and time permit a derived estimate; old support-bug audit measured contact-foot speed | Latest report has no validated contact-relative slip aggregate. Need ground/tray/material motion subtraction and contact gating. |
| Foot clearance [m] | Current plots show stance-minimum-referenced sole lift | Not true clearance above an evolving particle surface. Old custom report swing-height metrics aren't automatically valid for current native data. |
| Gait timing [s, Hz] | Raw sampled forces permit contact events. Old `report.py` calculates touchdown frequency, stride period/length and landing-force windows | Not fully migrated/validated in latest native summary; contact threshold/timing and 50 Hz limits need explicit definition. |
| Pelvis acceleration [m/s²] | Root/body-frame base velocity and angular velocity stored | No defined filtered world-frame pelvis acceleration series/aggregate. Differentiating rotating-frame velocity without rotation correction is invalid. |
| Cost of transport [dimensionless] | Applied torques stored | Not computed. Latest native records omit joint velocity needed for power integration; define positive/absolute/net mechanical work, distance, mass and gravity. Torque RMS is not CoT. |

Latest raw NPZ fields include time, root position, body-frame base linear/angular velocity, projected gravity, command, actions, applied torque, world foot forces, foot/sole positions and mass-weighted CoM; stance adds tray-relative CoM and tray joint angles; material adds density/friction. The old `report.py` includes a yaw-rate RMSE calculation for its archived dataset, but the latest `native_report.py` does not aggregate yaw tracking. A legacy helper's existence is not proof of a completed current metric.

## Proposed values for T3

Written 2026-11-19 by the T3 Claude session, for the task as redefined on 2026-11-18. Nothing was trained or evaluated for this section. Every value is a proposal awaiting Junnosuke's confirmation; `tasks/T3.md` is not edited.

Source of the facts below: the unified code at `~/isaac/worktrees/mpm-unification/IsaacLab`, branch `jnskkmhr/mpm-unification`, HEAD `65093be1c` with 138 uncommitted entries in the working tree (so "one commit" does not exist yet). Configs were read by instantiating `G1RoughEnvCfg` (rigid) and `G1MPMEnvCfg` (MPM) and their agent configs without starting a simulation. Status: read from code, not verified by a run or a second agent.

### Blocking findings

1. **The unified code has no RFT task and no RFT model.** `contrib/soft_contact/` does not exist there. `contrib/velocity/config/g1_29dof_soft_contact/` holds only `mdp/flat_observations.py` and `mdp/symmetry.py` and registers no task. RFT-Rough cannot be trained from the unified code until the model and task are ported. The last RFT task is `g1_29dof_soft_contact_mpm_align` in `~/isaac/worktrees/IsaacLab-T3` (`dcdc2ff3c`).
2. **The rigid task in the unified code is the older `g1_29dof_rigid` recipe, not the MPM-aligned one.** It differs from the MPM task in rewards, events, observations, terminations, curricula and robot asset (table below). The MPM-aligned rigid task (`g1_29dof_rigid_mpm_align`) is also only in `IsaacLab-T3`.
3. **The MPM task's default is 1 environment** (`G1MPMSceneCfg(num_envs=1)`); the rigid task's default is 4,096. The count must be set explicitly for every run.
4. **Slope units.** `slope_range` in the height-field terrains is rise over run, not an angle. The unified `Uphill` and `Downhill` test scenes pass `math.radians(10.0)` as rise over run, which is 9.9°, not 10°. The sweep must set `tan(angle)`.

### Open values

| Open value | Proposed value | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Training slope range and terrain shapes, per policy | Same generator for every policy: wave (amplitude 0.1–0.4 m, 4 waves per 8 m tile), random rough (noise 0.02–0.10 m), pyramid slope and inverted pyramid slope (rise/run 0–0.4, that is 0–21.8°), and flat; equal proportions, 8 × 8 m tiles, 10 difficulty rows, terrain curriculum on. Planar training range: 0–21.8°, uphill and downhill. The wave shape is steeper locally: from its formula, up to 32° along one axis and 42° diagonally at the top difficulty row. Which row each policy reaches must be read from its training log. | `ROUGH_TERRAINS_CFG` in `g1_29dof_rigid/env_cfg/terrain_cfg.py` and `velocity_mpm/config/g1_29dof/env_cfg/terrain_generator_cfg.py`; identical sub-terrains, 10 columns (rigid) vs 5 (MPM). Wave slopes computed from `hf_terrains.wave_terrain`, not from a generated mesh. RFT: not in the unified code. |
| Sweep angles, step, and maximum; uphill, downhill, or both | (Corrected 2026-11-25: the runs did not use a planar ramp but the training slope tile walked along its axis, a valley uphill and a ridge downhill; see "The slope tile is a valley".) Planar ramp, both directions, 5° steps: 0, ±5, ±10, ±15, ±20, ±25, ±30 (13 angles). ±5 to ±20 are inside the planar training range; ±25 and ±30 are beyond it. Maximum 30°, because a sand bed cannot hold a slope steeper than its angle of repose, atan(friction): 38.7° for the proposed evaluation sand. | Earlier pilot used 0, ±10, ±20, ±25, ±30 (`logs/rss_locomotion/final_rough_slope_20261115`, planning only); 5° steps add points inside the range. Repose limit is my reasoning, not measured. |
| Held-out terrain shapes, and why each counts as held out | (1) Tilting sand tray, roll and pitch ±10° and ±20°: the ground moves, and every training terrain is static. (2) Side-hill traverse at 25° and 30°: walking across the ramp, steeper than any trained planar slope. (3) Dune ridges, trough-to-crest 0.5 m, 3 m wavelength (peak slope 27.6°): one-directional and longer than the trained 2 m waves. The existing `Dune` test scene (0.3 m, 17.4° peak) is inside the trained wave range and is not held out. The training generator is broad, so (2) and (3) are held out by slope or wavelength, not by kind. | Test scenes `IsaacContrib-Velocity-G1-29dof-MPM-{Tilting-Balance,Tilting,Dune}` in `velocity_mpm/config/g1_29dof/`. Shapes (2) and (3) are not built yet. |
| Material parameters for each soft material, with the source of the values | Snow: density 250 kg/m³, friction 0.30, yield pressure 2.0e6 Pa, tensile yield ratio 0.05, hardening 1.0, dilatancy 1.0. Clay (sticky): density 1500 kg/m³, friction 0.0, yield stress 200 Pa, tensile yield ratio 1.0, dilatancy 0.1, viscosity 100. Powdery: **no preset and no source in the code**; candidate is loose poppy seed (640 kg/m³, friction 0.73), which is a light grain, not a powder. | `MATERIAL_PRESETS` in `velocity_mpm/config/g1_29dof/env_cfg/streaming_scene_cfg.py` (same values as the deprecated file named in the task). The code comment says snow and clay "start from the sand, snow, and mud rows of Table 5 in Daviet's mixed-MPM paper"; I did not check them against the paper. Poppy seed: Yang et al. (2020), cited in the same file. |
| Material range MPM-Rough is trained on, and whether the soft materials are inside it | Randomized at reset: friction 0.3–0.9 and density 1000–3000 kg/m³ (uniform), Young's modulus 1e7–1e9 Pa and yield pressure 1e8–1e9 Pa (log-uniform), Poisson ratio 0.3. Not randomized: tensile yield ratio, yield stress, hardening, dilatancy, viscosity (all 0). **All three soft materials are outside the range.** Snow: density, yield pressure (50 times below the minimum), tensile ratio, hardening, dilatancy. Clay: friction, yield stress, tensile ratio, dilatancy, viscosity. Loose poppy seed: density. | `G1EventCfg.mpm_material` and `mpm_material_log` in `velocity_mpm/config/g1_29dof/env_cfg/event_cfg.py`. Whether the solver stays stable with the snow and clay values at this voxel size and time step is untested; the same file notes an instability when randomizing the tensile yield ratio. |
| Evaluation sand for axes A and C | Density 1800 kg/m³, friction 0.80, Young's modulus 1e8 Pa, yield pressure 3.2e8 Pa, Poisson ratio 0.3, other parameters 0. All inside MPM-Rough's training range, so axis A varies terrain only. | Density and friction from the `beach_sand` preset (FHWA NHI-05-037 for density; friction is an uncalibrated tuning value). Modulus and yield pressure are the log-centres of the training range. Not fitted to the hardware sand. |
| Definitions of pelvis jerk and joint smoothness | Pelvis jerk [m/s³]: RMS over the episode of the norm of the third time derivative of the pelvis position, from the world-frame root linear velocity sampled at 50 Hz and differenced twice; first 1 s excluded, no filter. Joint smoothness [rad/s²]: RMS joint acceleration over the 29 joints, from joint velocity sampled at 50 Hz and differenced once. Action rate [rad/s] (RMS of 0.25 × action difference / 0.02 s) reported beside it. | My proposal. Joint acceleration matches the `dof_acc_l2` reward term; action rate is the earlier harness's definition. The earlier evaluation did not save joint velocity or world-frame root velocity, so both need new logging. |
| Commanded velocities; episodes and seeds per cell; success definition | Commands: forward 0.5 and 1.0 m/s, zero lateral and yaw rate; zero command on the tilting tray. Episodes: 10 per cell, reset seeds 41–50, one training seed per policy. Success: reaching the far end of the bed within the horizon with no fall termination; on the tilting tray, standing 30 s. Fall: base tilt above 45° or base too low. Side exits and timeouts count as not successful and are reported separately. Tracking errors are given over successful episodes, and separately over all episodes up to termination. | Earlier pilot: 1.0 m/s, 3 seeds, 13 s horizon. Fall terms from `G1TerminationsCfg` (`bad_orientation` limit 0.785 rad, `base_too_low`). Run time per trial on the unified code is not measured, so the cost of 10 episodes per cell is unknown. With one training seed there is no estimate of training variance. |
| Whether contact stiffness can be randomized per environment in MuJoCo Warp (for MJWarp-Rough-CR) | Yes in principle, not available today. MuJoCo Warp stores `geom_solref` per world, and Newton fills it from each shape's `ke` and `kd` and combines the two shapes of a contact. Each environment's foot shapes are separate, so their `ke` and `kd` can differ. The terrain is one shared shape, so only the foot side can vary, and the contact uses a mix of foot and terrain values. No Isaac Lab event does this; one would have to write the foot shapes' `shape_material_ke`/`kd` and notify the solver. Untested. | `mujoco_warp/_src/types.py` (`geom_solref: array("*", "ngeom", vec2)`); `newton/_src/solvers/mujoco/kernels.py` (`update_geom_properties`, force-space contact override); Newton at `6fee8b0b` in the unified workspace. Current fixed values: `ke` 160000, `kd` 1100. |
| Hardware test bed: material, dimensions, slope | **OPEN.** Nothing in this repo or the code states it. Needs Junnosuke. | — |

### How the training recipes differ in the unified code today

Rigid is `IsaacContrib-Velocity-Rough-G1-29dof-Rigid` (`g1_29dof_rigid/rough_env_cfg.py:G1RoughEnvCfg`, agent `rsl_rl_ppo_cfg.py:G1RoughPPORunnerCfg`). MPM is `IsaacContrib-Velocity-G1-29dof-MPM` (`velocity_mpm/config/g1_29dof/streaming_mpm_env_cfg.py:G1MPMEnvCfg`, default agent `compositional_ppo_cfg.py:G1MovingPatchPPORunnerCfg`). RFT has no task in the unified code; the column shows the last RFT task, `g1_29dof_soft_contact_mpm_align` in `IsaacLab-T3`, for reference only.

Identical in all three: control step 0.02 s (physics 0.005 s, decimation 4), episode 20 s, action scale 0.25 on 29 joints, command ranges (forward −1.0 to 1.5 m/s, lateral ±0.5 m/s, yaw ±1.0 rad/s, resampled every 10 s, 20% standing), command curriculum stages, policy history 10, observation noise on angular velocity, joint position and joint velocity, base-height target 0.75 m, foot friction randomization (static 0.6–1.0, dynamic 0.2–0.6), actuator gain scaling 0.9–1.1, pushes every 10–15 s at ±1 m/s, PPO hyperparameters (learning rate 1e-3, 5 epochs, 4 mini-batches, clip 0.2, entropy 0.005, γ 0.99, λ 0.95), network 512-256-128 ELU, 24 rollout steps, 20,000 iterations, symmetry data augmentation on.

| Parameter | Rigid (unified) | RFT (old task, reference) | MPM (unified) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Robot asset | `g1_29dof_rev_1_0.usd` | `…_box_foot_improved_collision.usda` | `…_box_foot_improved_collision.usda` |
| Default environments | 4,096 | 4,096 | 1 |
| Spawn height | 0.76 m | 0.76 m | 0.80 m |
| Terrain grid | 10 rows × 10 columns | 10 × 10 | 10 rows × 5 columns |
| Ground under the foot | rigid mesh | rigid and RFT columns | 0.25 m of sand over the mesh, voxel 0.03 m |
| Algorithm | PPO | PPO | compositional PPO (a standard PPO config also exists) |
| Mirror loss | 0.1 | 0.1 | off |
| `action_rate_l2_lower_body` | −0.01 | −0.05 | −0.05 |
| `ang_vel_xy_l2` | −0.05 | −0.25 | −0.25 |
| `lin_vel_z_l2` | −1.0 | −0.25 | −0.25 |
| `feet_air_time` | 0.5 | 2.0 | 2.0 |
| `no_fly` | −1.0 | −2.0 | −2.0 |
| `feet_pitch`, `feet_pitch_diff`, `feet_pitch_contact` | −4 each | absent | absent |
| `stance_foot_angle_penalty` | absent | −8.0 | −8.0 |
| `undesired_contacts` | −1.0 | absent | absent |
| `joint_deviation` hip-yaw weight | 0.15 | 0.08 | 0.08 |
| Tracking curriculum | reward std to 0.25 over 480,000 steps | std 1.0 to 0.316 over 360,000 steps, and tracking weight 4 to 8 | same as RFT |
| Projected gravity observation | uniform noise ±0.05 | bias up to ±3°, ramped over 120,000 steps | same as RFT |
| Command history | 0 | 1 | 1 |
| Critic quaternion | reordered (`root_quat_wxyz`) | `root_quat_w` | `root_quat_w` |
| Added base mass | −1 to 3 kg | 0 to 5 kg | 0 to 5 kg |
| Reset base velocity | 0 | ±0.5 on all six axes | ±0.5 on all six axes |
| Reset joint position scale | 1.0 | 0.5–1.5 | 0.5–1.5 |
| Terrain material randomization | none | RFT friction 0.1–1.0, stiffness 0.1–0.9, bulk density 1000–3000 | MPM material ranges in the table above |
| Fall tilt limit | 0.80 rad | 0.785 rad | 0.785 rad |
| Extra terminations | none | extreme action, solver diverged | extreme action, solver diverged |
| Leaving the terrain | time-out, 3.0 m buffer | failure, 0.5 m buffer | failure, 0.5 m margin |
| MuJoCo Warp substeps for the robot | 2 | 2 | 2, plus one MPM step per physics step |

Not resolved by reading configs: whether the MPM base-height and foot-clearance references are the sand surface or the terrain mesh 0.25 m below it (the MPM height scanner targets `/World/ground`, the rigid one `/World/ground/terrain`; MPM foot clearance uses a 0.25 m offset, base height does not); and the total environment count intended for MPM-Rough, which sets the transition budget. For reference, 20,000 iterations × 24 steps × 4,096 environments is 1.97e9 transitions.

### What has to happen before step 1

1. Commit the unified working tree, so all policies can be trained from one commit.
2. Port the RFT model and an RFT rough task into the unified code.
3. Decide the matched recipe. The MPM column above is the natural reference; the rigid task then needs the changes listed in the table, or the MPM-aligned rigid task ported from `IsaacLab-T3`.
4. Fix the environment count and iteration count per policy so the transition budgets are equal, and decide on mirror loss for all three.

Slope rows: M1 to M4 are velocity error [m/s], yaw-rate error [rad/s], slip [m/s] and sinkage [m], mean ± std over successful trials; M5 to M7 are pelvis jerk [m/s³], joint acceleration [rad/s²] and applied joint torque rate [N·m/s], mean ± std over all trials. Material rows: M1 and M2 are pelvis jerk [m/s³] and joint acceleration [rad/s²], mean ± std over all trials. Depth definition (P1 and P2): foot sinkage is the vertical depth of the sole below the settled sand surface while the foot is loaded. The settled surface of a trial is the generated terrain level lowered by the settling measured 0.6 m left and right of the pelvis on flat ground (column `sand_settle_m` of the per-trial table; medians: clay 9.1 cm, poppy seed loose 4.8 cm, snow 4.1 cm, sampled grid 4.4 cm, sampled l 4.4 cm, beach sand 4.3 cm), located by the highest sand particle, so accurate to about one particle spacing (2 cm). Until 2026-11-23 sinkage was measured from the generated level, which made every value deeper by the settling (about 4 cm on beach sand, 9 cm on clay); those values are replaced. No other P1 or P2 quantity uses a depth: step time and swing height are measured from each foot's own stance level. Raw data and per-trial table: `/home/jkamohara/isaac/worktrees/mpm-unification/IsaacLab/logs/t3/eval_5/report/trials.csv`.
