**Added 2026-11-25, 17:33, before computing: is the RFT policy less steady than the MPM policy? (asked by Junnosuke
after the videos: "3D RFT policy looks way more unstable than MPM. Is there any way we can quantify this?")**
No new runs: the rollouts of the final rerun. This is a description of how steadily the two policies walk; it
is not a test of why RFT falls.

- *What I have already seen, so this entry is not blind for it.* The whole-body comparison (entry of 01:23)
  already computed some of the measures below, and I know their outcome on the slopes: the spread of the pelvis
  roll and the RMS pelvis roll rate separate the policies with MPM the larger; the RMS pelvis pitch rate, yaw
  rate, velocity normal to the slope and action rate do not separate; the ranges of the waist and shoulder
  joints separate with RFT the larger. I have watched the clips. The measures marked "new" have not been
  computed.
- *Data and window.* As in the whole-body comparison: slopes +20°, +25°, +30° on beach sand, 40 trials per
  policy and slope, bed settings T (start on the settled surface) and W (12 s settling, sand awake), the
  default setting reported beside them; the five sands of the material ladder on level ground, 10 trials per
  cell, settings T and W. On a slope the window starts 0.3 m past the start of the slope, on level ground at
  1.5 s; a trial that does not reach the goal is cut 0.5 s before its end; a trial needs three gait cycles.
  RFT and MPM carry the reading; MJWarp is tabulated where it has trials.
- *Measures, per trial.* (1) RMS angular velocity of the pelvis about the roll, pitch and yaw axes of the
  heading frame, and of the three combined [deg/s]. (2) New: RMS angular velocity of the torso (pelvis
  angular velocity plus the three waist joints), three axes combined [deg/s]. (3) New: RMS linear acceleration
  of the pelvis, from the recorded velocity at 50 Hz, three axes combined [m/s²]. (4) Standard deviation of
  the pelvis pitch (new) and roll about their trial means [deg]. (5) New: step-to-step variability, the
  coefficient of variation over the steps of a trial of step time, step length along the slope and step
  width [%]. (6) New: lateral sway, the standard deviation of the pelvis position across the walking direction
  after removing a straight line over the window [cm]. (7) New: RMS joint velocity of the 14 arm joints and of
  the 3 waist joints [rad/s]. (8) RMS change of the action per control step, leg joints and all joints (the
  second is new). (9) New: position of the pelvis relative to the centre of the supporting sole during single
  support, along and across the walking direction: mean and standard deviation over the samples of a trial
  [cm] (the pelvis stands in for the centre of mass, which is not recorded). (10) The spread of the forward
  speed about its trial mean [m/s], from the whole-body comparison.
- *Two summary numbers, fixed now.* (A) trunk motion: measure (2). (B) step irregularity: the coefficient of
  variation of the step time. They are what a viewer would call unsteady: a trunk that swings and steps that
  are not alike.
- *Reading, fixed now.* A measure "separates" the policies on the slopes (on the sands) if RFT and MPM differ
  by at least two pooled standard deviations over trials, with the same sign, in T and in W, in each of the
  three slopes (five sands); the size is given as the ratio RFT over MPM per cell. "RFT walks less steadily
  than MPM on the slopes" is written only if (A) and (B) both separate with RFT the larger; if one separates,
  that one is named and the other is said not to; if neither, "not shown by these measures", and the measures
  that do separate are listed with their direction, whichever policy they favour. The same for the sands.
- *Relation to the falls, fixed now.* Reported for every measure that separates on the slopes, with the test
  of the entry of 01:23: among RFT's trials at +30°, over the first four gait cycles on the slope, area under
  the ROC curve of later falling against reaching the goal at least 0.70 pooled over the three settings with
  the task's sleeping and above 0.5 in at least two of them. A measure that does not pass is described as a
  difference in how the policies move, not as a reason for the falls.

**Added 2026-11-25, 17:48, before computing the trend: does RFT's unsteadiness build up with the slope, and faster
than MPM's? (asked by Junnosuke after the steadiness result)** No new runs. The question is about a trend over
the slopes; the data can show a trend, not its cause.

- *What I have already seen, so this entry is not blind for it.* The steadiness result above gave the values at
  +20°, +25°, +30° in the settings T, W and default, and on level beach sand in T: the torso's angular velocity
  rises with the slope for both policies (RFT 151, 159, 175 deg/s, MPM 101, 109, 121 deg/s in T; level 120 and
  76) and their ratio is about the same on every slope (1.4 to 1.6). So I expect two rising curves with a
  roughly constant ratio. Not seen: the cells at +5°, +10°, +15° and downhill, the other measures as curves,
  and any test within RFT's trials other than the one for the first four gait cycles at +30°.
- *Data.* Beach sand, 0.50 m bed, 1.0 m/s. Default bed setting: level and +5° to +30° in 5° steps, and −5° to
  −30° for reference; 10 trials per policy below +15° and downhill (seeds 41, 42), 40 trials from +15° (seeds 41
  to 48). Settings T and W: level (the beach-sand cell of the material ladder, 10 trials) and +15°, +20°, +25°,
  +30° (40 trials); there are no runs at +5° and +10° in T and W. The default setting carries the trend, T and
  W are the check. Window and trial rules as in the steadiness entry.
- *Measures against slope, per policy (RFT, MPM; MJWarp where at least three of its trials have a window).*
  The two summary numbers, (A) torso angular velocity (RMS) and (B) coefficient of variation of the step time;
  the spread of the forward speed; the lateral sway of the pelvis; the RMS velocity of the waist joints and of
  the arm joints. On the same axis, without a rule: goals reached and the velocity error of the trials that
  reach the goal.
- *(a) Does a measure rise with the slope for RFT?* Rank correlation of the cell means with the slope over the
  seven uphill cells of the default setting (level to +30°). "Rises" if it is at least 0.9 and the cell at +30°
  is above the level cell by at least two pooled standard deviations over trials. The same number is given for
  MPM, which is question (d).
- *(b) Does the gap between RFT and MPM grow?* Per slope the difference RFT minus MPM of the cell means and
  their ratio. Trend: the slope of a straight line through the differences against the slope angle, with a 95 %
  interval from resampling the trials inside every cell 2000 times; the same for the logarithm of the ratio.
  "The gap grows" if the interval of the difference's trend lies above zero in the default setting and the
  trend has the same sign in T and in W; "parallel" if the interval contains zero. If the difference grows and
  the ratio does not, that is said as "both rise in proportion".
- *(c) Do RFT's trials with more of a measure fall more often?* The test of the entry of 01:23, for each of the
  six measures: among RFT's trials at +30°, over the first four gait cycles on the slope, area under the ROC
  curve of later falling against reaching the goal at least 0.70 pooled over the three settings with the
  task's sleeping and above 0.5 in at least two of them. At +25° RFT fails in 2 of 160 trials over the four
  settings, so nothing can be tested there, and that is said.
- *(d) MPM.* (a) for the MPM policy, and for each measure the slope at which RFT's curve (straight lines
  between its cells) has the value that MPM has at +30°.
- *Reading, fixed now.* "RFT's unsteadiness builds up with the slope" is written if (a) holds for RFT for both
  summary numbers and (b) gives "the gap grows" for both; if that holds for one of the two, it is written for
  that one and the other is named as not rising or not growing. "It leads to RFT's failure" is written only if
  (c) passes for a measure. A constant gap is reported as "a constant difference between the two policies, not
  something that builds up toward the failure". Words about a cause (a mismatch of the model, compensation)
  are not used.

**Added 2026-11-25, 18:01, before any run: does RFT become unstable when it climbs for longer? (asked by Junnosuke:
"if you run policy longer on a longer slope, does RFT become unstable at the end? You can try 20 s at least")**
The slope trials so far end at the goal line after about 8 s, of which about 5 s are on the slope.

- *Runs.* RFT `6t0h2ud8` and MPM `aga5ovkp` on beach sand, 0.50 m bed, 1.0 m/s, footprint tracker on. The same
  slope tile as before, made larger (56 m instead of 16 m, so 26 m of slope ahead of the robot instead of 7 m),
  the goal line moved out of reach (25 m past the start of the slope) and the horizon set to 23 s, so that a
  trial that stays up ends by time after at least 20 s on the slope. Everything else as in the merged slope
  runs: default bed setting (start with the drop, 2.5 s settling, the task's sleeping) at +20°, +25°, +30°,
  and setting W (12 s settling, sand kept awake) at +25° and +30°; 40 trials per cell (seeds 41 to 48, five
  robots per run, each on its own tile and its own sand). MJWarp is not run (it falls within 4 s on these
  slopes). Before the runs a local check has to show that the particle patch and the rigid base 0.50 m below
  the surface follow the robot to the end of a 23 s trial; it is reported with the runs.
- *Time axis.* Time on the slope, from the moment the pelvis is 0.3 m past the start of the slope, in 2 s bins
  up to 20 s. A fall is a termination of the task (pelvis tilted past 45° or too low); a trial that leaves
  its lane is counted as lost to observation at that time, not as a fall.
- *Measured per bin, over the trials still up:* share of trials still up (survival curve); forward speed, mean
  and spread inside the bin; trunk motion (RMS angular velocity of the torso); pelvis pitch; share of the
  samples with a knee at or above 95 % of its torque limit; sand motion under the sole per stance; foot
  sinkage at mid-stance. For the trials that fall: the time of the fall.
- *Reading for the fall rate, fixed now.* Falls per robot-second at risk in the first 10 s on the slope against
  the second 10 s. With n falls in all, the number in the second half is compared with a binomial
  distribution whose share is the second half's share of the robot-seconds at risk (two-sided). "The fall
  rate rises with time" if the second half's rate is the higher and p is below 0.01; "falls early" if it is
  the lower and p is below 0.01; otherwise "a constant fall rate is not rejected". Fewer than 5 falls in a
  cell: no statement about the rate.
- *Reading for drift, fixed now.* For the trials that are up for the whole 20 s: the value over 14 to 20 s
  minus the value over 2 to 8 s (the first 2 s on the slope are the transition from level ground), per trial.
  A measure "drifts" for a policy if the mean of these differences is at least one standard deviation over
  trials of the 2 to 8 s value, upward for the measures of unsteadiness (forward-speed spread, trunk motion).
  As a check that does not depend on surviving, for every trial with at least 6 s on the slope the slope of a
  straight line through its 2 s bins (the last second before a fall left out) is given per 10 s.
- *Statements.* "RFT becomes unstable with time on the slope" is written for a slope and bed setting if RFT's
  fall rate rises with time, or if its forward-speed spread or trunk motion drifts while MPM's does not.
  "No drift" is written if a constant fall rate is not rejected and neither measure drifts. Anything else is
  described as found.
- *+20° and +25°, fixed now.* RFT reaches the 8 s goal there in 39 to 40 of 40. Reported: the share still up
  after 8 s on the slope and after 20 s. "Still stands after 20 s" if at least 37 of 40 are up at 20 s;
  "does not keep climbing: the earlier result was a statement about 8 s" if at most 32 of 40 are up at 20 s
  and that differs from the share up at 8 s in the same runs (Fisher's exact test, p below 0.01); 33 to 36 is
  undecided.
- *What it would and would not mean.* A fall rate that is constant in time means that falls arrive at a steady
  rate and a longer climb only gives them more time; a rising rate or a drift would mean that something
  builds up. Neither says why.

**Added 2026-11-25, 19:20, before any run: the slope tile is a valley, the long runs move to a planar ramp, and the
merged slope runs get a planar counterpart (amendment to the entry of 18:01, which is left as written).**

- *What was found in the local check of the long tile.* The slope tile of all slope runs so far is the training
  sub-terrain, Isaac Lab's pyramid slope, whose height is the product of two ramps (`hf_raw = height_max * xx *
  yy`). The walking test sends the robot from the platform along the tile's axis, the crease of that surface:
  uphill the robot climbs the floor of a V-shaped valley, the ground rising to both sides; downhill it walks
  along a ridge. Measured from the surface heights recorded under the feet in the scored runs (16 m tile, MPM):
  at +30° the slope along the walk is 29.7°, 29.2° and 28.8° at 3, 5 and 6.5 m from the tile centre, and the
  slope across, under each foot and rising outward, is 19 to 20°, 11° and 6° (the formula gives 19.8°, 12.2°,
  6.2°); at +20° the slope across is 12.6°, 7.4° and 3.6°.
- *Why the design of 18:01 is withdrawn.* Making the tile larger changes the valley: on a 56 m tile the side
  slopes are 27° at 3 m and 20° at 10 m. In the local check 3 of 3 MPM robots fell there (5.6, 8.8, 15.2 s,
  forward, sand and its depth in place), while the MPM policy has not fallen once on the 16 m tile. The 56 m
  tile is a different, harder terrain and is not used. These local trials are not results.
- *New terrain: a planar ramp with a level approach* (`scripts/benchmarks/t3/ramp_terrain.py`, new;
  `eval_walk.py --terrain ramp --approach 1.87`). A level strip on which the robot starts, then a plane that
  rises along the walk with no slope across; the start of the slope is 1.87 m ahead of the start, as on the
  16 m tile. Checked locally at +30°: the recorded surface has 30.0° along and 0.00° across; three MPM
  robots reach the goal, two of three RFT robots do. A start on the ramp itself was tried and dropped: three of
  three RFT robots fall within 2 s of a standing start on a 30° ramp, which would test the start and not the
  climb. So, differently from what I proposed, the robot starts on the level strip in both items below.
- *Item A, the planar counterpart of the merged slope runs.* MJWarp `3qymp37c`, RFT `6t0h2ud8`, MPM `aga5ovkp`
  on the planar ramp at +20°, +25°, +30°; everything else as in the merged runs of the default bed setting:
  beach sand, 0.50 m bed, 1.0 m/s, goal line 5 m past the start of the slope, horizon 13 s, lane half-width
  1.5 m, footprint tracker on, 40 trials per cell (seeds 41 to 48, five robots per run, each on its own lane
  and its own sand). MJWarp alone also at +10° and +15°, to see where it stops climbing.
- *Reading of A, fixed now.* Per policy and slope: goals of 40, kinds of failure, velocity error of the trials
  that reach the goal, beside the valley's numbers (default setting, goals at +20°, +25°, +30°: MJWarp 0, 0, 0;
  RFT 40, 39, 13; MPM 40, 40, 40). MPM against RFT per slope by the reading of the entry of 02:54 (success:
  Fisher's exact test, p below 0.01; velocity error: 0.05 m/s and two pooled standard deviations where both
  reach 10 goals). *"The slope result holds on a planar slope"* if the three success verdicts are the valley's
  (not separated at +20° and +25°, MPM better at +30°); otherwise the slopes that differ are named. Per policy
  and slope, *"the valley changes the outcome"* if the goals on the planar ramp differ from the valley's with
  Fisher's exact test p below 0.01; otherwise "no change shown". MJWarp: the steepest slope with at least 37
  of 40 goals and the shallowest with at most 3, beside the valley's (10 of 10 at level, 9 of 10 at +5°, 4 of
  10 at +10°, 0 of 40 from +15°).
- *Item B, the long runs.* As in the entry of 18:01 with the terrain replaced: RFT and MPM on the planar ramp,
  slope 25 m long, horizon 23 s, lanes 12 m wide (a robot is lost to observation 5.5 m to the side), the
  particle store sized for the strip a robot walks; default setting at +20°, +25°, +30° and setting W at +25°
  and +30°; 40 trials per cell (seeds 41 to 50, four robots per run, since a robot takes about 9 GB of GPU
  memory on this tile). The reading of 18:01 holds unchanged: fall rate of the second 10 s against the first
  10 s, drift from 2 to 8 s against 14 to 20 s, and for +20° and +25° the share still up after 8 s and after
  20 s.
- *Order.* Item A at +30° and +25° first, since it says whether the headline holds on a planar slope.
- *What this does not change.* The merged slope numbers stay as measured; what changes is their description:
  they are results on the training slope tile walked along its axis, not on a planar slope.
- *Added 2026-11-25, 20:41, after check 11 (corrections to the entry of 19:20, which is left as written).* (1) The
  formula values for the side slope in that entry (19.8°, 12.2°, 6.2°) use a half-width of 8 m; the sloped part
  of the tile is 15.5 m wide (half-width 7.7 m), which gives 19.4°, 11.4°, 5.1° at +30° and 12.5°, 7.3°, 3.3° at
  +20°. (2) "Lane half-width 1.5 m" is the side limit; the planar mesh is 4 m wide, the tile 16 m. (3) The
  local trials named in that entry (MPM on the 56 m tile, the standing start on a 30° ramp) are local trials,
  not results. (4) "No change shown" by the rule is not "no effect": for RFT at +30° the difference planar
  minus tile is +12 points with a 95 % interval of −9 to +32 points.

**Added 2026-11-25, 20:42, before the run: is the change of code bundle between the tile runs and the planar runs
neutral? (asked by the eleventh check)** The merged runs on the slope tile used bundle `bd2f5052f974fca8`, the
planar runs bundle `1eb8610adc89fadf`; `eval_walk.py` gained options in between, none of which is meant to
change a run that does not set them.

- *Run.* RFT `6t0h2ud8` on the slope tile at +30°, exactly as the merged default run (16 m tile, 13 s, five
  robots per run, seeds 41 to 48, 40 trials, footprint tracker on), with bundle `1eb8610adc89fadf`. RFT at +30°
  is the cell with the most room to move (13 of 40 in the merged run).
- *Reading, fixed now.* "No change shown" if the goals of 40 do not differ from the merged run's 13 of 40 by
  Fisher's exact test at 0.01; the difference is given in points with its 95 % interval, and the first recorded
  pelvis position and joint angles of each trial are compared with those of the merged run (they have to be
  equal). "The bundle changes the result" if p is below 0.01; the planar comparison would then have to be
  redone on one bundle. Reruns with the same starts are known to part after about a second (eighth check), so
  equal counts are not expected trial by trial.

**Added 2026-11-25, 20:45, before any run: the shin touches the foot's contact margin on MPM sand and stops the
ankle; rerun without that contact (asked by the lead after check 3 of T5).**

- *The finding, and what I verified myself before writing this.* In the MPM tasks the collision shapes of the
  two feet carry a contact margin of 2 cm (`FOOT_CONTACT_MARGIN = 0.02`, set on `*_ankle_roll_link` by the
  robot's `collision_props` in `velocity_mpm/config/g1_29dof/env_cfg/streaming_scene_cfg.py`). The sand needs
  it, but the rigid contact between the robot's own links reads the same margin, so the shin (`knee_link`)
  meets the foot 2 cm early and the ankle pitch joint cannot go beyond about −0.46 rad, 26 of its 50 degrees
  toes-up. The rigid and the RFT tasks have no such margin. In my own rollouts on MPM sand (slope tile and
  planar ramp at +30°, planar +10°, level beach sand; three policies) no ankle sample is below −0.51 rad, the
  1st percentile is −0.44 to −0.48 rad in every cell, and 33 to 93 % of the samples lie between −0.50 and
  −0.40 rad. The checker's local trials (3 per cell, +30°: MPM 0 of 3 without the contact, 3 of 3 with it; RFT
  2 of 3 both ways) are not results. I have seen them; this reading is written knowing them.
- *What is changed, in the evaluation only.* A new option of `eval_walk.py`, off by default: when the model is
  built, the shape pairs (`knee_link` shapes, `ankle_roll_link` shapes) of each leg of each robot are added to
  the collision filter (`builder.add_shape_collision_filter_pair`). Nothing else changes: the margin stays for
  the sand and for every other contact; the task code is not touched. Each run records how many pairs were
  filtered (2 per robot expected) and the ankle pitch reached. No policy is retrained.
- *Cells.* MJWarp `3qymp37c`, RFT `6t0h2ud8`, MPM `aga5ovkp`; beach sand, 0.50 m bed, 1.0 m/s, default bed
  setting, footprint tracker on. Planar ramp with the level approach at +10°, +20°, +30° and the slope tile at
  +20°, +30°: 40 trials per cell (seeds 41 to 48, five robots per run). Level ground on beach sand and on loose
  poppy seed: 10 trials per cell (seeds 41, 42). Every cell is run twice in one workflow, on the same GPU
  type: with the contact as it is (control) and with the pair filtered.
- *Does the filter act?* In the filtered arm the 1st percentile of the ankle pitch has to lie below −0.50 rad
  for a policy; if it does not, that cell tests nothing for that policy and is reported so.
- *Reading per policy and cell, fixed now.* Goals with the contact against goals without it: "the shin-foot
  contact changes the outcome" if Fisher's exact test gives p below 0.01, otherwise "no change shown", with the
  difference in points and its 95 % interval (Newcombe). Reported beside it: kinds of failure, velocity error
  of the trials that reach the goal, ankle pitch (1st, 5th percentile, median), the sole's angle to the
  ground in loaded stance, sand motion under the sole.
- *Reading for the comparison of the policies, fixed now.* In the filtered arm, MPM against RFT per cell by the
  reading of the entry of 02:54 (success: Fisher's exact test at 0.01; velocity error: 0.05 m/s and two
  pooled standard deviations where both reach 10 goals). *"The separation of MPM from RFT at +30° holds without
  the shin-foot contact"* if MPM is better at +30° on the planar ramp and on the tile in the filtered arm.
  *"The separation at +30° was the shin-foot contact"* if in the filtered arm MPM is not better than RFT at +30°
  on both terrains and the contact changes the MPM policy's outcome there. Anything else is reported terrain
  by terrain. *MJWarp:* "its failure on this ground was the shin-foot contact" for a cell in which the contact
  changes its outcome upward; "not the contact" for a cell with at most 3 goals in both arms; other cells are
  given with their numbers.
- *What a result would and would not mean.* The MPM policy was trained with this contact and the baselines
  without it, so the filtered arm is the fairer test of the baselines and an unfair one of the MPM policy,
  which may rest on a stop that is no longer there. If the MPM policy fails without the contact, that shows
  that this policy as trained needs it; a comparison of training methods then needs an MPM policy trained
  without the contact, which is the user's decision and is not started here. If nothing changes, the merged
  comparisons stand as they are.

<!-- T3-slope-tile:start -->
## The slope tile is a valley, not a planar slope (found 2026-11-25)

Status: found in this session while checking a longer tile; measured from the recorded surface heights of the
scored runs; reported to the lead; not yet checked by a fresh agent. A planar counterpart of the merged slope
runs is running (definitions block, entry of 2026-11-25, 19:20).

**What the tile is.** Every slope run of T3 so far uses the training sub-terrain as its slope:
`HfInvertedPyramidSlopedTerrainCfg` uphill and `HfPyramidSlopedTerrainCfg` downhill (Isaac Lab,
`isaaclab/terrains/height_field/hf_terrains.py`), one 16 m tile per robot, platform 2 m. The height of that tile
is the product of two ramps, `hf_raw = height_max * xx * yy`, with `xx` and `yy` falling linearly from 1 at the
tile centre to 0 at its edges. It is a planar slope only on the two axes through the centre. The walking test
starts the robot on the platform and sends it along the +X axis, which is a crease of the surface: uphill the
robot climbs the floor of a V-shaped valley, the ground rising to both sides; downhill it walks along a ridge,
the ground falling to both sides. Along the axis the slope is the nominal one; a foot standing beside the axis
is on ground that also slopes sideways by `slope * xx`, largest near the platform and falling to zero at the
tile edge.

**Measured in the scored runs** (surface heights recorded under the four corners of each sole; 16 m tile, MPM
policy, default bed setting):

| Nominal slope | Distance from the tile centre | Slope along the walk | Slope across, under a foot, rising outward | Product formula (sloped part 15.5 m wide, half-width 7.7 m) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| +30° | 3 m | 29.7° | 19 to 20° | 19.4° |
| +30° | 5 m | 29.2° | 11° | 11.4° |
| +30° | 6.5 m (the goal line is at 6.87 m) | 28.8° | 6° | 5.1° |
| +20° | 3 m | 19.4° | 12.6° | 12.5° |
| +20° | 5 m | 19.3° | 7.4° | 7.3° |
| +20° | 6.5 m | 19.3° | 3.6° | 3.3° |

So the angle along the walk is within about 1° of the nominal angle, and each foot stands on a side slope that
faces inward and flattens as the robot climbs. The slope along the walk is a little below nominal because the
robots walk 0.2 to 0.4 m beside the axis.

**What used this tile.** The slope sweep in every bed setting and bed depth (level ground is the same tile
with zero slope and is plane); the slope-by-friction grid; the sole, sand-flow and close-up analyses on slopes;
the whole-body comparison, the last seconds before RFT's falls, the steadiness measures and their trend
against slope; the swing-height and knee-below-the-surface numbers on slopes; the knee, hip pitch and ankle
torque-limit runs and the baselines with knee limits; the side-view clips at +20°, +25°, +30°. The analyses
of sand motion on slopes measure motion along the walking direction only; sand moving toward the valley floor
is not in them. Not affected: the material ladder and the density-friction grid (level ground), and the
towing runs of T2. The rigid-ground slope runs were made with a different test, `eval_rigid.py`, whose files do
not record the terrain; by its code it builds the same two pyramid tiles (found by the eleventh check), so the
rigid-ground slope results are on the valley tile too.

**Training.** The three policies trained on the same tile family: 8 m tiles, pyramid and inverted pyramid at 20 %
of the tiles each, slope 0 to 0.4 (21.8°) with the terrain level (MPM task
`velocity_mpm/config/g1_29dof/env_cfg/terrain_generator_cfg.py`; RFT and MJWarp tasks, baselines worktree,
`g1_29dof_soft_contact_mpm_align` and `g1_29dof_rigid_mpm_align`, `env_cfg/terrain_cfg.py`; present code, not
the runs' logged configurations). In training a robot starts on the platform with a random heading, so it
crosses the tile in any direction and meets the valley floor or the ridge only when it walks along an axis.
"Trained on slopes up to 21.8°" therefore means tiles whose slope along their axes is at most 21.8°; the
steepest ground on such a tile is at the platform's corner on the diagonal, 1.04 times the nominal slope
(22.5°; half-width of the sloped part 3.75 m). No policy was trained on a planar slope.

**What this changes.** The merged slope numbers stay as measured. Their description changes: they are results
on the training slope tile at a nominal angle, walked along its axis, not on a planar slope. Whether the valley
matters for the comparison of the policies is not known until the planar runs are in. One earlier planning row
of this file ("Proposed values for T3", sweep angles) says "planar ramp"; the runs did not use one, and the row
is corrected there. A larger tile is not a longer slope of the same kind: on a 56 m tile the side slopes are
27° at 3 m and 20° at 10 m, and in a local check 3 of 3 MPM robots fell there (local trials, not a result). A
standing start on a planar 30° ramp was also tried locally: 3 of 3 RFT robots fell within 2 s (1.5, 1.8, 1.5 s)
and 3 of 3 MPM robots walked for the whole 23 s; these are local trials, not a result. The planar runs
therefore start on a level strip, as the tile does.

**MJWarp at +10° against the hardware: what differs, as far as this session knows (the lead asked for the
list; nothing has been run on it).** In this simulation the MJWarp policy reaches 7 of 40 goals on a planar
+10° slope (4 of 10 on the tile) and none from +15°, while the task file says that on hardware "on flat ground
and a 10 degree slope every policy succeeded". What the simulated trial is: forward command 1.0 m/s with no
turning; 1.87 m of level sand, then 5 m of slope, goal line at its end, horizon 13 s; the robot is placed above
the sand and dropped onto it (default bed setting; the sand is settled for 2.5 s before, settled sand is put
to sleep by the task and wakes around the robot); beach sand as set in the task (density 1800 kg/m³, friction
0.80, no cohesion), 0.50 m deep over a rigid base; only the two feet touch sand (a 17 x 6 x 1 cm plate each),
shins and knees pass through it; checkpoint `3qymp37c` at iteration 19,999; no pushes, no mass, friction or
gain randomization in the test; a fall is the pelvis tilting past 45° or sinking to 0.2 m above the feet. Of
its 33 failures at +10°, 28 are the pelvis sinking too low and 5 the pelvis tilting, half of them within 5.3 s.
What is known to depend on the test in this simulation: the bed setting. At +15° MJWarp reaches 0 of 40 with
the task's sleeping (three settings) and 23 of 40 with the sand settled for 12 s and kept awake; at +10° only
the default setting was run (planar 7 of 40, tile 4 of 10), so the setting closest to a settled real bed has
not been run at 10°. What this session does not know about the hardware trial: the commanded speed, the
length of the slope and how success was counted, the number of trials, the sand (kind, depth, how it was
prepared, dry or damp), which checkpoint of which training run was on the robot, and the robot's actuator
limits. Any of these could carry the difference; none was checked.
<!-- T3-slope-tile:end -->

## P1-1 terrain geometry on a planar slope: counterpart of the slope sweep (2026-11-25)

Status: checked on 2026-11-25, eleventh check: every planar number reproduced from the raw files; corrections applied. Data: `logs/t3/final_1/planar/report/planar_compare.json`; scripts `scripts/benchmarks/t3/planar_compare.py`, `planar_slides.py`, terrain `ramp_terrain.py`.

<b>The same slope runs on a planar slope (checked on 2026-11-25, eleventh check: every planar number reproduced from the raw files; corrections applied; reading committed before the runs, task record, definitions block, entry of 2026-11-25, 19:20).</b> The slope tile of every other slope result on these slides is the training tile, a valley along the walk (conditions line above). Here the three policies walk a planar ramp: a level strip, then a plane rising along the walk with no slope across; the slope starts 1.87 m ahead of the start, the goal line is 5 m further, the horizon is 13 s and a robot more than 1.5 m to the side has left its lane, as on the tile (the planar mesh is 4 m wide, the tile 16 m). Beach sand, 0.50 m bed, 1.0 m/s, default bed setting, 40 trials per cell (seeds 41 to 48, five robots per run, each on its own lane and sand). Goals of 40 on the planar slope, with the slope tile's count in brackets: +20°: MJWarp 0 / 40 (0), RFT 40 / 40 (40), MPM 40 / 40 (40); +25°: MJWarp 0 / 40 (0), RFT 40 / 40 (39), MPM 40 / 40 (40); +30°: MJWarp 0 / 40 (0), RFT 18 / 40 (13), MPM 40 / 40 (40). <b>By the rule: the slope result holds on a planar slope.</b> No terrain effect is shown for any policy and slope by the rule (Fisher's exact test at 0.01). "No change shown" is not "no effect": the largest difference between RFT's or MPM's counts is RFT at +30°, 18 against 13 of 40, +12 points with a 95 % interval of -9 to +32 points, so an effect of the tile of that size is not excluded; it cannot remove the gap to MPM (40 of 40). MJWarp, run alone on the shallower planar slopes: 7 / 40 at +10° (slope tile 4 / 10), 0 / 40 at +15° (slope tile 0 / 40); it reaches at most 3 of 40 goals from +15° on, as on the slope tile, so the slope at which it stops climbing does not move. ## The slope and level runs with and without the shin-foot contact (2026-11-25)

Status: unchecked. Data: `logs/t3/final_1/shin_contact/report/shin_contact.json`; scripts `scripts/benchmarks/t3/shin_contact_compare.py`, `shin_slides.py`, option `eval_walk.py --no_shin_foot_contact` (`shin_foot_filter.py`).

<b>The same runs with and without the shin-foot contact (unchecked; reading committed before the runs, task record, definitions block, entry of 2026-11-25, 20:45).</b> In the MPM tasks the feet's collision shapes carry a contact margin of 2 cm, which the sand needs. The contact between the robot's own links reads the same margin, so the shin meets the foot 2 cm early and the ankle pitch joint stops at about −0.46 rad, 26 of its 50 degrees toes-up. This holds for every run on MPM sand on these slides, and for the training of the MPM policy; the RFT and the rigid training have no such stop. Here every cell is run twice in one workflow on the same GPU type: as it is, and with that one contact removed in the evaluation (the shin and foot shapes of each leg are added to the collision filter; margin, sand contact and every other contact unchanged; no policy retrained). Beach sand unless named, 0.50 m bed, 1.0 m/s, default bed setting, 40 trials per cell and arm. Goals with the contact → without it: slope tile, +30°: MJWarp 0 → 0, RFT 13 → 28, MPM 40 → 1. <b>The +30° cells of both terrains are not in yet, so the rule for the separation of MPM from RFT is not applied yet.</b> By the rule the contact changes the outcome for RFT on the slope tile, +30° (13 to 28 of 40); MPM on the slope tile, +30° (40 to 1 of 40). The MPM policy was trained with this contact and the two baselines without it, so the runs without it are the fairer test of the baselines and an unfair one of the MPM policy. A comparison of training methods without the contact needs an MPM policy trained without it; that was not done. In so far: slope tile, +30°; the rest is running.

| Ground | MPM against RFT with the contact: success | velocity error | MPM against RFT without it: success | velocity error |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| slope tile, +30° | MPM better (p = 2e-11) | not separated | RFT better (p = 9e-11) | not compared |

| Ground | Policy | Goals with the contact | Goals without it | By the rule | Ankle pitch, 1st percentile, with / without [rad] | Ankle pitch, median, with / without [rad] | Failures without the contact | Velocity error of the trials that reach the goal, with / without [m/s] | Sole angle to the ground in mid-stance, toes up, with / without [°] | Sand under the sole per stance, with / without [cm] |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| slope tile, +30° | MJWarp | 0 / 40 | 0 / 40 | no change shown (p = 1.00; +0 points, 95 % interval -9 to +9) | -0.44 / -0.86 | -0.34 / -0.45 | 23 fall forward, 1 fall sideways, 16 collapse | — / — | -69.1 / -61.4 | 8.1 / 12.1 |
| slope tile, +30° | RFT | 13 / 40 | 28 / 40 | the shin-foot contact changes the outcome (p = 2e-03; +37 points, 95 % interval +16 to +55) | -0.45 / -0.89 | -0.40 / -0.67 | 12 fall forward | 0.193 / 0.203 | -50.8 / -37.1 | 6.4 / 7.4 |
| slope tile, +30° | MPM | 40 / 40 | 1 / 40 | the shin-foot contact changes the outcome (p = 8e-22; -98 points, 95 % interval -100 to -84) | -0.46 / -0.91 | -0.43 / -0.64 | 39 fall forward | 0.130 / 0.531 | -48.1 / -62.1 | 5.1 / 12.0 |

<!-- T3-shin-notes:start -->
### Notes to the shin-foot runs (hand-written, 2026-11-25, unchecked)

- *Status of the cells.* In: slope tile +30° (8 workflows `t3-shin-t30-s41…48-1`, each with the control task
  `beach-sand-p30` and the filtered task `beach-sand-p30-noshin` on NVIDIA L40, code bundle `300131cc8bc652ef`).
  Running or queued, in this order: planar ramp +30°, slope tile +20°, planar ramp +20°, level beach sand and
  loose poppy seed, planar ramp +10°. The rule for the separation of MPM from RFT at +30° needs the planar cell
  and is not applied yet.
- *Does the option act, and only as meant?* Every filtered run records 10 filtered pairs of colliding shapes on
  5 robots (2 per robot: one shin shape against one foot shape per leg); the run stops if the count differs.
  With the contact the 1st percentile of the ankle pitch is −0.44, −0.45, −0.46 rad (MJWarp, RFT, MPM); without it
  −0.86, −0.89, −0.91 rad. The two arms of a seed start from identical states (first recorded pelvis position and
  joint angles equal for all 120 trials) and part within 0.02 to 0.08 s: the contact is active from the first
  control step of every run on MPM sand. Local check before the runs (three robots, RFT, planar ramp +30°): 6
  pairs on 3 robots, ankle pitch down to −0.905 rad; those three trials are not a result. A run that renders
  video also carries the links' visual shapes (8 pairs per robot, of which 2 collide); the check counts the
  colliding pairs.
- *What the tile +30° cell says, by the reading fixed before the runs.* The shin-foot contact changes the
  outcome of RFT (13 → 28 of 40, p = 0.0016, +37 points, 95 % interval +16 to +55) and of MPM (40 → 1 of 40,
  p = 8e-22, −98 points, interval −100 to −84); for MJWarp "not the contact" (0 and 0 of 40). Without the contact
  39 of the 40 MPM trials fall forward, at a median of 3.8 s and a median farthest pelvis position of 3.5 m
  (the slope starts at 1.87 m); RFT's 12 failures are falls forward. Without the contact RFT is better than MPM
  by the rule (28 against 1, p = 9e-11).
- *What it does not say.* The MPM policy was trained with this contact, the baselines without it. The filtered
  arm is the fairer test of the baselines and an unfair one of the MPM policy: it shows that this policy as
  trained needs the contact at +30° on the tile, not that training on particles gives no advantage there. That
  question needs an MPM policy trained without the contact; a retraining is the user's decision and is not
  started. Every earlier statement on these pages about the MPM policy on slopes, and every statement about RFT
  and MJWarp on MPM sand, was measured with the contact; none of them is changed here until the planar cell is
  in and a check has run.
- *Is the change of code bundle neutral? (reading of 20:42, read on the control arm of this cell as agreed with
  the lead, in place of the separate rerun).* The control arm ran on bundle `300131cc8bc652ef`, which holds the
  changes of `1eb8610adc89fadf` and the new option, off in this arm; the merged runs on `bd2f5052f974fca8`.
  Goals of 40: MJWarp 0, RFT 13, MPM 40 against the merged 0, 13, 40. RFT: 0 points, 95 % interval −20 to +20,
  Fisher p = 1: "no change shown". The first recorded pelvis position and joint angles of all 120 trials equal
  those of the merged run once the seeds are matched (the merged file holds the seeds in the order 43 to 48,
  41, 42). The bundle named in the reading, `1eb8610adc89fadf`, was not rerun by itself.
- *Long climb (reading of 18:01, amendment of 19:20), first wave only, on hold.* Planar ramp +30°, default bed
  setting, 25 m of slope, 23 s, 40 trials (seeds 41 to 50, four robots per run), with the contact: all 40 RFT
  trials fall, the last at 14.3 s and 11.6 m up the slope (22 of 40 pass the 5 m line of the short runs, which
  count 18 of 40); 38 of 40 MPM trials walk at 23 s, and no measure drifts by the rule. The MPM half of this
  run rests on the contact as well. The other waves (+25°, +20°, setting W) are not submitted: whether they run
  with the contact, without it or both is the user's decision. Data `logs/t3/final_1/planar/long_default`,
  report `logs/t3/final_1/planar/long_report`; not on the slides.
- *Videos, so that the falls can be seen (one robot per run, slope tile +30°, start of seed 41, side view, sand
  drawn as particles, without the shin-foot contact; rendered locally with the same option, 2 pairs of colliding
  shapes filtered).* `site/T3/media/shin30_without_contact_mpm_retrain_seed41.mp4`: the MPM policy walks the
  level strip, sinks into a deep crouch on the first metres of the slope and goes down forward onto its knees

  `site/T3/media/shin30_without_contact_mjwarp_seed41.mp4`: MJWarp falls at 3.4 s. These are single trials that
  agree with the counts of 40; they are not linked from the slides yet. The clips with the contact for the same
  slope are the ones on the slides (`gait_slope30_*`).

**Failures and what was done.** Every failure was the same: the sand template did not settle to below 0.03 m/s
in the 2.5 s allowed (0.030–0.044 m/s). It hit the 500 kg/m³ sand on the 0.50 m bed and, by a hair
(0.0304–0.0313 m/s), dense sand and both poppy seeds on some beds of the ladder. Fix: 6 s settling for those
cells of the material axis and for the whole ladder (rerun once, as agreed for overnight runs; no second
failure). 0.50 m and 0.65 m runs use 5 and 4 trials per run because 10 do not fit in the memory of one L40
(local measurement: 14.6 GB for 2 trials at 0.50 m against 10.8 GB at 0.35 m; the local GPU was shared, so this
is rough).

**The four issues of `checks/T3.md`.**

1. *Explanation and headline on different beds*: closed. The headline sweep and the explanation (sand motion,
   sole angle, failure types, close-up, schematic) are both from the 0.50 m bed, all three policies tracked in
   the same workflows.
2. *MPM against RFT rests on an invalid cell*: closed. On the 0.50 m bed +30° is clear of the base (0 of 40 RFT
   trials and 1 of 40 MPM trials within 2 cm) and MPM reaches 40 of 40 against 13 of 40 (Fisher p = 2e-11). At
   +25° the two are not separated (40 against 39 of 40) and the slides say so.
3. *The 2 cm rule is not validated*: closed by dropping the rule. It was tested on 75 cells between the 0.35 and
   0.50 m beds with tolerances fixed beforehand and does not pick out the cells that change; results are quoted
   from the deepest bed instead. Open remainder: MJWarp's results keep getting worse with depth, and loose poppy
   seed still changes between 0.50 and 0.65 m.
4. *Coverage and provenance*: closed for MPM on the material axis and on the grid, for provenance in the result
   files and blocks, for the training budget (W&B configs) and for the rigid-ground reference (rerun on the
   cluster with raw data). Not closed: one training seed and one checkpoint per policy; trial start states on the
   0.50 and 0.65 m beds are not the same as on the shallower beds.

Small flaws of the check: base height now read under each sole corner; "every trial" wording replaced by counts.

**Steep slopes, RFT against MPM, step by step (record only; not on the slides).** 40 trials per policy and
slope, per-stance medians, 0.35 m / 0.50 m bed.

| Peak tangential load [body weights] | 0.75 / 0.78 | 1.25 / 1.25 | 0.94 / 0.94 | 1.52 / 1.52 | 1.29 / 1.45 | 1.85 / 1.86 |

- *"MPM makes the sand yield less"*: supported by the rule fixed beforehand. On the 0.35 m bed, per seed, the
  sand under the sole moves 2.06–2.20 cm (MPM) against 3.26–3.30 cm (RFT) at +20° and 3.44–3.57 against
  4.33–4.63 cm at +25°: lower in every seed, by about 1 cm, which is more than RFT's range between seeds
  (0.04 and 0.30 cm).
- Peak load, corrected after the recheck (`checks/T3.md`, N6): the peak loads in the table are taken from the
  first 50 Hz sample above 20 N on, which leaves out the up to 15 ms in which the foot lands. With that interval
  included RFT's median peak normal load is 1.52–1.57 body weights at +25° and 2.20–2.40 at +30°, as high as or
  higher than MPM's 2.04–2.10. So "MPM pushes harder than RFT" holds at +20° only (1.9 against 1.3 body
  weights) and is not a general statement.
- What differs in the body: MPM leans further forward and carries the pelvis 3–4 cm further ahead of the loaded
  foot at +20° and +25°. RFT's failures are falls forward (30 of 30 on 0.35 m; 25 of 27 on 0.50 m).
- This is a description. Which of these differences keeps MPM up at +30° is not established.

**Three things about the simulated bed that this rerun found** (details in the definitions block and in the
material block): the settling time changes the bed a little (6 s: surface about 1 cm lower, feet sink 10–20 %
less); the default start drops the robot 4 to 16 cm onto the settled surface, more on deeper beds, which alone
decides RFT's result on loose poppy seed on the 0.65 m bed (0 of 12 with the drop, 9 of 12 without); and on
some deep beds the settled sand does not yield under the feet (dense sand on 0.50 m for MJWarp and on 0.65 m
for every policy, beach sand on 0.65 m in 22 of 36 trials), cause not established.

### After the recheck of the final rerun (2026-11-24, runs unchecked)

The recheck (`checks/T3.md`, "rerun" sections) reproduced every number and raised the points N1 to N11. What was
done about them, in the order the lead asked for:

1. *What makes the sand stop yielding (N1).* Not sleeping alone: the bed keeps losing height while it is awake,
   and sleeping freezes it in whatever state it has. Diagnostic table and reading in the definitions block
   ("two bed configurations"). The ladder with 6 s settling (ladders B and C above) is withdrawn as a result.
   The post-hoc threshold for "sand that does not yield" is no longer used on any figure.
2. *One setting per comparison (N2, N4).* The material part is now on one bed (0.50 m), one start (on the settled
   surface, measured by each task before its trials) and one velocity-error definition (from 1.0 s, trials that
   reach the goal), run in two bed settings, T (2.5 s settling, the task's sleeping) and W (12 s settling, all
   sand near the robot awake). Statements are made only where T and W agree. The first material axis is no
   longer shown.
3. *Grid settled as beach sand (N8).* The density-friction grid was rerun with every sand generated and settled as
   itself (`osmo_workflow.py --sand_grid`), in T and in W. The 3D mesh figures come from the T run; sands whose
   bed collapses while settling are marked instead of the 2 cm rule (N3 of the earlier list, item 6).
4. *Slopes without the start drop (N3).* Steep slopes on the 0.50 m bed, 40 trials per policy, started on the
   settled surface with 2.5 s and with 6 s settling, and in setting W. Goals at +30° (MJWarp / RFT / MPM): 0 / 13
   / 40 with the default start; 0 / 9 / 40 and 0 / 18 / 40 on the surface; 0 / 1 / 40 in W. MPM against RFT is
   separated at +30° in every setting (p from 8e-9 to 8e-22) and not at +25° (39 or 40 against 40). The
   criterion written beforehand is met by RFT and MPM everywhere; MJWarp at +15° reaches 0 of 40 with the task's
   sleeping and 23 of 40 in W, so where MJWarp starts to fail depends on the setting. Sand under the sole per
   stance, RFT against MPM, median: 3.5 against 2.2 cm at +20° and 4.5 against 3.2 cm at +25° on the surface
   with 2.5 s settling; 2.6 against 1.5 cm and 4.2 against 2.7 cm in W. So "less sand moves under MPM's sole"
   holds in both.
5. *Two statements corrected (N5, N6).* RFT's stance is not shorter than MPM's on dense sand or hard ground; the
   longer stance of MPM appears on the three softest grounds only. "MPM pushes harder" holds at +20° only
   (corrected above).
6. *Markers and count (N3 remainder, N11).* The 2 cm markers are off the grid figure; 41 workflows were in the
   first table, not 43.
7. *Wording (lead's summary).* The P1-2 slide now says: MPM against MJWarp is supported; MPM against RFT is not
   separated in goals, and RFT's velocity error is 0.03 to 0.08 m/s higher on every sand regardless of softness.

**Workflows after the recheck** (bundle `bundles/49857bdd83468b97`, Isaac Lab `0007b2e67` with 76 uncommitted
entries, Newton `48231a98`; it adds `eval_walk.py --sleep` to bundle 2): `t3-final-surf50-s41…48-1-1` and
`t3-final-surf50six-s41…48-1-1` (steep slopes on the surface), `t3-final-steepW-s41…48-1-1`,
`t3-final-ladderT-s41/42-1-1`, `t3-final-ladderW-s41/42-1-1`, `t3-final-gridT-1-1`, `t3-final-gridW-1-1`. All
completed except grid cells with friction 0.1 (T: 250 and 500 kg/m³ did not settle to 0.06 m/s; W: six of seven
densities collapsed onto the floor), which are reported as collapsed beds. Data roots under
`logs/t3/final_1/`: `surf50`, `surf50six`, `steepW`, `ladderT`, `ladderW`, `gridT`, `gridW`; diagnostic runs in
`logs/t3/settle_diag/` and `logs/t3/settle_probe/`.

**Low-friction beds and the empty-cell explanation (lead's question).** In the grid the settled surface depends on
the friction only and is the same for every density: 8.3, 11.6, 15.0, 20.8, 25.1 and 30.1 cm below the generated
level for friction 0.9, 0.7, 0.5, 0.3, 0.2 and 0.1 after 2.5 s (10.0, 13.7, 18.1, 27.0, 32.7 and 37.9 cm after
12 s). Empty grid cells at the sampling density (T1's finding, 12 to 14 % of the cells) would give the same loss
for every friction, about 6 to 7 cm of a 50 cm bed if filling them removed that share of the height. So the
empty cells can account for most of the loss at friction 0.9, not for the three to four times larger loss at low
friction. A second, friction-dependent cause is needed; the bed spreading sideways toward its angle of repose
is a candidate that fits "friction only, not density", and it is not tested.

**Paper figures after the recheck** (new files; the three `_final` material files of this morning are
superseded, the slope and tow files stand): `t3_material_final2.pdf` (3D mesh, grid in setting T, 0.50 m bed,
each sand settled as itself, collapsed beds marked), `t3_yielding_final2.pdf` (slope and that mesh),
`t3_material_ladder_final2.pdf` (ladder in setting T).

### Sand flow under the foot across materials (2026-11-24, checked: pass with caveats)

Requested by Junnosuke through the lead; measures and criterion are in the definitions block (three entries of
this afternoon and one correction after the check). The result, its tables and the test are in the block "P1-2
terrain material", part "Sand flow under the foot"; on the slides it is the third figure of P1-2. Check 4 in
`checks/T3.md` reproduced every number from the raw downloads and asked for the rewording below; the values as
volume of the settled bed and the values without the side region taken off were added after it and are not
checked again.

- *Runs.* The material ladder repeated in settings W and T with a tracker that compares every footprint before
  and after (`foot_sand.py`: `column_change`, `region_budget`, arrays `stance_col_*`): `t3-final-flow2W-s41/42-1-1`
  and `t3-final-flow2T-s41/42-1-1` (bundle `bundles/1a9aaf78bfb030a3`, Isaac Lab `0007b2e67` with 76 uncommitted
  entries, Newton `48231a98`), used for every number; `t3-final-flowW/T-s41/42-1-1` (bundle
  `bundles/c9caf3e1c88bb919`), submitted minutes earlier without the region budget, give the same column values.
  All eight completed. Data roots `logs/t3/final_1/flow2W`, `flow2T`, `flowW`, `flowT`; analysis
  `scripts/benchmarks/t3/material_flow.py`. A trial is one start of a robot; each robot starts on its own
  copy of the same settled sand; 10 trials per policy and ground.
- *First attempt, no result.* The particle boxes saved by the earlier ladder runs hold 0 to 3 footprints per
  cell that the foot has already left (sand is simulated only within 0.8 m of the pelvis), so the split could
  not be taken from them.
- *Answer.* More sand leaves the footprint column on softer ground, for every policy, in both settings (W: 0.16
  to 0.18 L on dense sand, 1.33 to 1.53 L on loose poppy seed, litres at nominal density; as volume of the
  settled bed 0.12 to 0.13 and 0.92 to 1.03 L). It does not come up beside the foot. In the region around the
  footprint in setting W, 65 to 88 % of what sinks is compaction (65–83 % with the region to the side taken
  off, 72–84 % without, 80–88 % with the flow as volume of the settled bed); this does not hold for the column
  under the sole (flow is 37–70 % of the rut as volume of the settled bed) and not in setting T as reported.
  Whether degradation follows flow is **not shown, and not separable from sinkage**: MPM does not degrade, RFT's
  error is flat, and for MJWarp the rank correlation is 0.70 in W over 5 grounds and 1.00 in T over 4 grounds
  (0.8 in both was required), with the same values for sinkage on the same grounds; the grounds where MJWarp
  falls in every trial have no footprint and enter neither this test nor the test between policies.
- *Supported, narrower.* On the softest ground where it still steps (light sand in W: 15 footprints from 7
  trials; packed poppy seed in T: 129 footprints) MJWarp moves about a third to a half more sand out of the
  footprint column and sinks 40 to 80 % deeper than RFT and MPM. On packed poppy seed in W it moves 63–69 % more
  out of the column without failing (10 of 10, 0.07 m/s) and not more out of the region. On dense and beach
  sand the three policies move the same sand while MJWarp and RFT track 0.05 to 0.08 m/s worse than MPM, so flow
  does not account for that gap.
- *Method caveats from the check*, all stated with the result: the unit (above); the region to the side is not
  undisturbed (W: sinks 0.44–0.85 L as the robot passes; T: gains 0.1–0.6 L of sand, so taking it off overstates
  T's flow); the budget closes by construction; the criterion was first committed after the results.
- *While these ran the disk filled up* (1.1 GB free at 13:07); one bundle failed on it and the bundles and the
  first downloads were staged in `/dev/shm` until Junnosuke freed space. Nothing was deleted by this session;
  the three policy checkpoints were confirmed present with unchanged hashes afterwards.
- *Paper figure*: `t3_material_flow_final2.pdf` (new file; same plot, panel titles now carry the unit and "side
  region taken off"). `t3_material_flow_final.pdf` is superseded and left in place.

### How forces are recorded: towing, dragged cube, feet (2026-11-24, unchecked)

Asked by the lead after T1 found that its box probe reads the sand force only at the last of the four physics
steps of a control step, where it is lowest. Read from the code and the saved files; no new runs.

- *Towing (tether tension and ground force on the cube): not affected.* The tension is the held value that
  acted: `Tether.apply()` computes the rope force once per control step and holds it for all four physics steps,
  and that value is logged. The ground force on the cube is never read from the sand solver: it is mass times
  acceleration (cube velocity at the control samples, 0.2 s average) minus the rope force minus weight
  (`tow_report.box_ground_force`), so it is the mean over the physics steps by construction, on MPM sand, RFT
  sand and rigid ground alike. The tow-in-a-channel runs use the same two records.
- *Dragged level cube (block "A. Controlled box test"): affected in kind, probably 5 to 10 % low, not proven.*
  Details are with that test.
- *Foot loads on MPM sand.* The control-rate record is read after the last physics step. The runs with the
  footprint tracker also hold the force after every physics step, so the difference was measured on the final
  rerun (54 files): vertical load in stance, last step against the mean of the four, 0.98 to 1.005 in every
  cell; share of time a foot is loaded the same within 0.01. Stance detection, sinkage and goals are not
  affected, and the slope explanation's load time, loading rate and peak load already use the physics-rate
  record. The horizontal load read at the last step is 0.86 to 1.02 of the mean of the four (MJWarp policy 0.96
  to 1.02, RFT 0.86 to 1.01, MPM 0.86 to 0.99).
- *Two columns recomputed.* "Peak tangential load" and "tangential / normal" of the material ladder are now
  taken from every physics step (`material_behaviour.py`; hard ground keeps the contact sensor's control-rate
  values). The peak rises by 4 to 63 % (largest for RFT: 0.19 to 0.31 body weights on light sand in setting W),
  the ratio changes by −7 to +13 %. The earlier values are kept in the table beside the new ones. No quantity
  passes the explanation rule before or after, and no statement rested on these two columns. Peak normal load
  and loading rate of that table, and the peak tangential load of the steep-slope comparison
  (`steep_compare.py`), are still read once per control step.

### Slope explanation slide: sole angle and close-ups in one figure (2026-11-24)

Requested by Junnosuke through the lead, in three steps the same afternoon. The sole-angle plot (formerly
Figure 15) and the small particle close-ups at +20° (formerly Figure 13) are one figure now (Figure 13 of the
page; later figures moved down by one). First version: three panels in a row under the plot. Second: the
close-ups as insets on the plot's own axes. Third, current: the same with a third less height.

- One axes. Lines, bands, level line and training range of the sole-angle plot are unchanged; the y-axis runs
  from −84° to +60° so that the insets fit in the free areas (RFT and MPM above the curves, MJWarp below), and
  the legend and the colour bar sit in the lower left and upper left corners.
- Insets: trial and frame of the old close-ups (median trial, 5th of 10 by distance per second; first frame
  1.5 m up the slope, 0.1 s after the foot takes load), equal aspect, no ticks, foot collider drawn as its box
  (17 by 1 cm) with the ankle as a dot. Frame and leader arrow have the policy's colour and the arrow ends on
  that policy's point at +20°. RFT's and MPM's points almost coincide there (−26° and −28°), so RFT's arrow
  comes from the upper left and MPM's from the far right.
- Labels: the sole angle in the frame shown, MJWarp −83°, RFT −27°, MPM −32°; the points the arrows end on are
  the means over all stances (−57°, −26°, −28°). MJWarp's median trial at +20° is a fall (0 of 10 reach the
  goal), which the caption says. +20° was kept as asked; no slope would show a sole-angle difference between
  RFT and MPM, and for MJWarp +10° would show a frame from a trial closer to its usual stepping.
- Sizes: slide figure 9.6 by 4.3 in (second version 9.6 by 6.4 in); paper figure 3.45 by 1.80 in (second
  version 3.45 by 2.69 in), single column, insets 0.74 by 0.48 in.
- No new simulation. Code: `slope_flow_report.py` (`closeup_insets`, `sole_closeup`, option `--redraw`),
  `slope_flow_slides.py`, `paper_figures.py --sole_closeup logs/t3/final_1/d50 --suffix _final3`. Figure
  `logs/t3/final_1/d50/flow_report/slope_sole_closeup.png`. Paper files, each a new file:
  `t3_sole_closeup_final3.pdf` (current), `t3_sole_closeup_final2.pdf` (insets, taller) and
  `t3_sole_closeup_final.pdf` (panels in a row), the last two superseded and left in place.
- The drawing (Figure 12) now largely repeats the insets and the large close-up (Figure 14, in the details)
  shows the same three frames; both stay until Junnosuke decides.
- *Axis labels, later the same day (Junnosuke's wording):* x "Terrain slope [deg]", y "Foot angle wrt ground
  [deg]" on one line, on the page figure and in the paper file `t3_sole_closeup_final5.pdf` (same size and
  data; `final4` has the x label only). The y value is still the angle of the sole to the slope surface in
  mid-stance, toes up positive, and "level sole" still means a horizontal sole; the captions have to say so,
  as well as that negative slopes are downhill. Alternative beside it, for him to choose:
  `t3_sole_closeup_final5_alt.pdf`, in which MJWarp's inset reads "MJWarp: −83° (fall)", because with a shorter
  caption nothing else explains why that inset (−83°) differs from the point its arrow ends on (−57°).

### After the fifth check (slope explanation, 2026-11-24)

Check 5 in `checks/T3.md` reproduced every number of the slope explanation from the raw downloads and rejected
one reading. Changed in the slope explanation block and on the slide, no new runs:

- *Withdrawn:* "MPM's foot does not move less, the sand under it does", with RFT's foot carried by the sand
  and MPM's sliding 3.1–3.5 cm over it. The whole-stance motion of the bearing point is dig-in motion plus sand
  motion plus a remainder, and the two totals at +20° (4.5 and 4.2 cm) are close only by cancellation; MPM's
  remainder is its toe moving in a lightly loaded end of stance; at the sole centre neither foot slides
  downhill; with the sand kept awake the contrast vanishes at +15°. The whole-stance figure moved from the
  slide into the collapsed details, and the table column is now called "bearing point minus that sand
  (remainder)".
- *Stands:* under MPM's sole the sand moves 20 to 40 % less than under RFT's from +15° to +30°, in every seed
  and in all four bed settings. No measured behaviour explains it.
- *Rank correlation* of sand motion with slope: quoted as "above 0.9 for every policy" (0.96, 0.99, 0.99 with
  tied ranks averaged). `spearman()` in `slope_flow_report.py` now averages tied ranks; the saved
  `slope_flow.json` still holds the earlier values and is not shown anywhere.
- *Which trials:* failed trials are included up to the fall. MJWarp: 6 trials at +10° and +20°, 7 at +15°, 2
  trials and 6 stances at +30°, none at +25°; RFT at +30° includes its 7 failing trials. Said with the sand-
  motion table and in the captions of Figures 8 and 13.
- *Bed setting:* MJWarp on level ground differs with the sand kept awake (sole 15° instead of 24° toes-down,
  bearing-point speed 0.29 instead of 0.54 m/s); +10° exists in the default setting only. "MJWarp's foot never
  comes to rest" and an order between RFT and MPM at +30° are no longer claimed.

## Values for the paper's walking table, with the stance foot velocity (2026-11-24, unchecked)

Status: computed in this session from the existing result files, no new runs, not independently checked.
Requested by Junnosuke through the lead for `tab:t3_walk`. Files for the paper session:
[site/T3/data/t3_walk_table.csv](../site/T3/data/t3_walk_table.csv) (every number, both material settings) and
[site/T3/data/t3_walk_table_rows.tex](../site/T3/data/t3_walk_table_rows.tex) (rows in the table's layout).
Script: `scripts/benchmarks/t3/walk_table.py` (unified worktree); output `logs/t3/final_1/walk_table/`.

**Stance foot velocity, definition.** Horizontal speed of the centre of the sole between two consecutive control
samples (50 Hz) in both of which that foot carries more than 20 N, averaged over all such sample pairs of both
feet, from 1.0 s on (on a slope: from 0.3 m past the edge of the start platform), then averaged over the
trials that reach the goal. This is exactly the "contact foot velocity" panel of the earlier slope and
material figures (`foot_slip_m_s` of `summarize_walk.py`; the slope values reproduce `geometry.json`). One
sentence for the caption: "Stance foot velocity is the mean horizontal speed of the sole centre over the
control samples in which the foot carries more than 20 N."

**It is not the bearing-point speed of the fifth check.** That quantity follows the lowest of heel, middle and
toe along the surface from the first to the last sample above 20 N of each stance, over all trials with at
least three stances. It is given in the last column for comparison (0.54 / 0.11 / 0.16 m/s on level beach sand,
0.84 and 0.70 m/s for RFT and MPM at +30°, as the check confirmed). The two agree within 0.05 m/s on most
cells and differ where the sole pitches during stance (RFT at +30°: 0.56 against 0.84 m/s).

**Conventions, the same as for e_v and step time in the table.** Mean ± standard deviation over the trials
that reach the goal, number of trials in brackets; a dash where no trial reaches the goal. A trial is one
start of a robot, each robot on its own copy of the same settled sand (position ±0.1 m, yaw ±0.1 rad, joints ±10 %); 10 trials per
cell (seeds 41 and 42), 0.50 m bed, 1.0 m/s command, final checkpoints. Step time here is one over the step
frequency counted from the ankle height, for every row.

**Bed settings.** Slopes: the setting of the slope sweep (default start, which drops the robot onto the settled
surface; 2.5 s settling; the task's sleeping). Materials: setting T (start on the settled surface, 2.5 s
settling, the task's sleeping), the one the table's SR and e_v values come from. Hard ground is rigid contact
without sand.

**What to know before using the column.**

- *Cells that rest on few or failing trials.* MJWarp reaches no goal at +20° and +30° and on (1000, 0.70) and
  (640, 0.73), so those cells are dashes under the table's convention. Its foot does touch the sand there: over
  all trials up to the fall the value is 1.10 m/s at +20° (10 failing trials, 94 stances), 1.20 m/s at +30°
  (10, 76), 1.42 m/s on (1000, 0.70) (10, 38) and 1.48 m/s on (640, 0.73) (10, 24); these are a falling robot's
  feet and should not be read as stance. MJWarp at +10° rests on 4 trials (all 10 trials: 0.94 m/s), on
  (682, 1.07) on 9. RFT at +30° rests on 3 trials (all 10 trials: 0.66 m/s).
- *The value depends on the bed setting much more than SR and e_v do.* In setting W (12 s settling, sand kept
  awake) the stance foot velocity on the sands is 0.3 to 1.0 times the value in T (table below); only the two
  cells on dense sand for RFT and MPM agree within 0.05 m/s. The order between the policies is the same in
  both: MJWarp highest on every sand where it walks, RFT and MPM close on the firm sands, MPM above RFT on the
  two softest.
- *SR and e_v disagree between T and W* for MJWarp on (682, 1.07) (90 % and 0.48 m/s in T, 100 % and 0.06 m/s
  in W) and on (1000, 0.70) (0 % in T, 100 % and 0.39 m/s in W). RFT at +30° is 30 % here (3 of 10); over the
  four bed preparations with 40 trials it is 1 to 18 of 40.
- *Higher is not simply worse.* On the soft sands MPM's stance foot velocity is above RFT's (0.40 against
  0.23, 0.53 against 0.41, 0.85 against 0.64 m/s in T) while its e_v is lower. A foot counts as in stance from
  20 N, which includes landing, lift-off and a lightly loaded toe (fifth check), and the value is read from
  the force at the last physics step of each control step. I would give the column no better/worse arrow.
- *Step time of the material rows.* The current table's material rows use the step time from touchdown to
  touchdown of opposite feet (`material_behaviour.json`), its slope rows the count from the ankle height. The
  rows here use the second for all; the material rows differ from the current table by at most 0.02 s
  ((682, 1.07): 0.22 / 0.24 / 0.27 against 0.20 / 0.24 / 0.26). The CSV has both.
- *Hard ground.* 0.01 m/s for every policy: the foot does not move while it is loaded.

### Paper figures as a pair of 1 by 3 plots (2026-11-24, stance foot velocity unchecked)

Requested by Junnosuke through the lead: the slope and the material figure of the paper with the same three
panels in one row. New files in the paper's `figures/`, nothing replaced, no `.tex` touched:
`t3_slope_1x3_final.pdf` and `t3_material_1x3_final.pdf`. Both 3.45 by 1.42 in (single column), same panel
rectangles, fonts, policy colours, y-axis ranges and legend place (lower left of the first panel).

- *Panels, left to right:* success rate [%]; velocity tracking error [m/s]; stance foot velocity [m/s]. The
  error and the stance foot velocity are means over the trials that reach the goal, band one standard
  deviation; where a policy has no such trial it has no point (as in the earlier figures, which carry no extra
  mark either; the success rate panel shows the 0 %).
- *Slope figure:* x = slope angle, −30° to +30° in 5° steps, beach sand (1800 kg/m³, 0.80), 0.50 m bed, 10
  trials per policy and slope. Bed setting: the slope sweep's default (the robot is dropped onto the settled
  surface; 2.5 s settling; the task's sleeping). Values from `logs/t3/final_1/d50/geometry/geometry.json`, the
  data of `t3_slope_full_final3.pdf`; grey marks the slopes seen in training.
- *Material figure:* x = the six grounds from stiff to soft, labelled rigid, (3000, 0.90), (1800, 0.80),
  (682, 1.07), (1000, 0.70), (640, 0.73) as (density [kg/m³], friction); level ground, 0.50 m bed, 10 trials per
  policy and ground. Bed setting: T (start on the settled surface, 2.5 s settling, the task's sleeping); hard
  ground is rigid contact. Values from the table block above (`logs/t3/final_1/walk_table/t3_walk_table.json`).
  In this setting the two figures differ in the start (drop against settled surface), so their common ground,
  level beach sand, is not the same run: e_v 0.10 / 0.10 / 0.06 against 0.10 / 0.09 / 0.06 m/s, stance foot
  velocity 0.53 / 0.12 / 0.13 against 0.51 / 0.12 / 0.14 m/s.
- *Stance foot velocity, one sentence for the caption:* "Stance foot velocity is the mean horizontal speed of
  the sole centre over the control samples in which the foot carries more than 20 N, averaged over the trials
  that reach the goal." It is the quantity of the "contact foot velocity" panel of the earlier slope figure,
  the same definition in both figures; it is not independently checked, and it is not the bearing-point speed
  of the fifth check.
- *What a reader should not take from the third panel:* the value depends on the bed setting (in setting W it
  is 0.3 to 1.0 times the T value on the sands) and higher is not simply worse (on the soft sands MPM's is
  above RFT's while its error is lower).
- *On the page:* both shown beside the current figures of the slope and the material slide, labelled
  "Proposed paper figure", outside the figure numbering so the existing numbers stay.
- Code: `paper_figures.py --pair logs/t3/final_1/d50/geometry/geometry.json
  logs/t3/final_1/walk_table/t3_walk_table.json --suffix _final` (`pair_figure`, `slope_1x3`, `material_1x3`).
- *Second version, same afternoon:* the x label of the slope figure is "Terrain slope [deg]" (the note "< 0:
  downhill" is gone from the axis and has to be in the caption), and the third panel's title has 1 pt more
  room on the right. Same size and data. Files `t3_slope_1x3_final2.pdf` and `t3_material_1x3_final2.pdf`; the
  sole-angle figure has the same x label in `t3_sole_closeup_final4.pdf`. The earlier files are left in place.
- *Merged version (evening of 2026-11-24):* `t3_walk_2x3_final.pdf`, 3.45 by 2.38 in, single column: the slope
  row on top and the material row below, with the same panels, lines, bands, colours and y-ranges as the two
  1 by 3 files (which rendered identically before and after the code was restructured for it). Column titles
  once above the top row, one legend in the first panel, "Terrain slope [deg]" under the top row and "Ground
  material (ρ [kg/m³], μ)" under the bottom row. The two separate figures take 2.84 in together. Bed settings
  as stated above: top row default start with the drop, bottom row setting T. On the page it is shown first,
  before the two 1 by 3 figures (`site_slides.py --paper-merged`).

### Alternatives of the paper's material figure: density axis, and 3D (2026-11-24, unchecked)

Asked by Junnosuke through the lead: could the material figure be a 3D plot, or is 2D easier to read? Two
alternatives were drawn beside `t3_material_1x3_final2.pdf`, as new files in the paper's `figures/`; nothing
was replaced, no new runs, and they have not been handed to the paper session.

- *Data for both:* the density-friction grid on level ground, 0.50 m bed, setting T (start on the settled
  surface, 2.5 s settling, the task's sleeping), each sand settled as itself, 5 trials per sand and policy
  (`logs/t3/final_1/gridT`). Sands whose bed holds: friction 0.7 and 0.9 in T and in W, friction 0.5 in T only.
  The stance foot velocity does exist for the grid runs (`foot_slip_m_s` in `gridT/report/trials.csv`, the same
  definition as in the 1 by 3 pair); it is not independently checked.
- *A, `t3_material_1x3_density.pdf`* (3.45 by 1.42 in, the pair's panel rectangles, fonts and colours): success
  rate, velocity error and stance foot velocity against bulk density on a log axis, stiff on the left as in the
  material figure, hard ground as a separate marker left of 3000 kg/m³ (from the ladder's rigid row). One line
  per policy for friction 0.9 (solid, filled markers) and friction 0.7 (dashed, hollow markers): 14 sands, 7
  densities from 250 to 3000 kg/m³. Each point is 5 trials for the success rate; error and stance foot velocity
  are over the trials that reach the goal, which is 5 everywhere except MJWarp at 1000 kg/m³ with friction 0.9
  (3 trials) and at 1500 kg/m³ with friction 0.7 (1 trial), and it has no point at 500 kg/m³ (friction 0.9) and
  1000 kg/m³ (friction 0.7), where it reaches no goal. The y-ranges of the two right panels are wider than in
  the pair (0 to 0.8 m/s and 0 to 1.6 m/s instead of 0 to 0.6 and 0 to 1.05), because the grid reaches softer
  sands than the six grounds.
- *B, `t3_material_1x3_3d.pdf`* (3.45 by 1.42 in): the same three quantities as 3D surfaces over density and
  friction in the style of the earlier mesh figure, sands whose bed holds in T (friction 0.5, 0.7, 0.9; 21
  sands), crosses on the floor where the bed collapses.
- *What the grid shows that the six grounds do not:* MJWarp's success is not monotonic in density (goals of 5
  at 3000 … 250 kg/m³: friction 0.9: 5, 5, 5, 5, 3, 0, 5; friction 0.7: 5, 5, 5, 1, 0, 5, 5); RFT and MPM reach
  5 of 5 on all 14 sands; RFT's error lies above MPM's on every sand; the stance foot velocity rises steeply
  below 1000 kg/m³ for every policy.
- *My view on readability at 3.45 in:* A is readable but busier than the six-ground figure (six lines per
  panel); the hollow dashed lines of friction 0.7 lie close to the solid ones except for MJWarp. B is not
  readable at this width: three overlapping translucent surfaces per panel, tick labels of 4 pt, and with
  three friction values the surface is a strip. The six-ground figure remains the clearest; A is the option if
  a continuous axis is wanted.
- Code: `paper_figures.py --material_alt logs/t3/final_1/gridT logs/t3/final_1/gridT/collapse.json
  logs/t3/final_1/walk_table/t3_walk_table.json` (`material_density_1x3`, `material_3d`); on the page both are
  shown after the proposed material figure as "Alternative A" and "Alternative B" (`site_slides.py
  --material-alt`).
<!-- T3-walk-table:end -->
